# Changelog

All notable changes to SimpleVTT are documented in this file.
Format: [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) — Versioning: [SemVer](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

The current version is the topmost release section below.
Application version and database schema version are also published at runtime by `GET /version` and `GET /healthz`, and are defined as constants in [`app/version.py`](app/version.py).

> For pre-2.0.0 history, see [CHANGELOG_v1.md](CHANGELOG_v1.md).

---

## [2.23.0] - 2026-05-19

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase T.8 — chat-card per-target rendering + roll-toast target suffix.** Surfaces the target_name field that T.1 added to the action broadcasts. The roll-log chat cards (`weapon_attack`, `spell_cast`, `feature_used`) now render a crimson `→ NAME` tag next to the action name when a target was set. The full-sheet's roll-toast (`.atk-strike` handler) also appends `→ NAME` to its note so the rolling player sees the target in their own popup, not just in the chat log. /attack broadcast + response gain a `target_name` field (resolved server-side from the hub combatant) — other targeting endpoints already include it. New `_lookup_combatant_name` helper in `tabletop_routes.py` is the resolver. Pure UX polish; no contract changes. MINOR — new visible UI surface (target tags + extended toast text).
**Description:** Five edits. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new `_lookup_combatant_name(campaign_id, combatant_id)` helper that scans the hub battle state for the matching combatant id and returns its display name (empty string when not in init or no battle). **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/attack` — payload + response gain a `target_name` field populated via the new helper. **(3)** `app/static/tabletop.js` — new `_targetTagHtml(d)` helper builds the `<span class="target-tag">→ NAME</span>` markup from the broadcast's `target_name` field; injected into `appendSpellCast`, `appendWeaponAttack`, and `_appendFeatureUsed`. **(4)** `app/templates/tabletop.html` — `.target-tag` CSS (crimson palette matching the in-canvas target ring + the floating targeting chip so the chat card visually ties back to the token on the map). **(5)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `.atk-strike` handler — builds a `tgtSuffix = ' → ' + data.target_name` string and appends it to both the attack-roll and damage-roll toast notes (`🎯 Greataxe → Vex — attack`). **(6)** `tests/harness/test_attack_buff_intercepts.py` — new `test_attack_broadcast_includes_target_name` test seeding a combatant with the real roster name and asserting `/attack` response carries `target_name`. Suite grows 128 → 129.
**Description (cont):** Why crimson tag color. Matches the in-canvas target ring (`#dc2626`) + the floating "🎯 Targeting" chip from T.0. A player who double-clicks a token sees the same crimson visual cue in three places: the ring around the token on the map, the chip at the bottom of the map pane, and the tag on the eventual chat-log card. The triple-tie makes "what got targeted" hard to miss.
**Description (cont 2):** Why /attack needed `target_name` added server-side. Phase B (v2.20.0) added `target_combatant_id` to the /attack broadcast but not `target_name` — the chat card would have needed a second client-side lookup against the hub state to resolve the name. The new `_lookup_combatant_name` helper centralizes that resolution server-side; the chat card just reads `d.target_name` uniformly across all action types. Other targeting endpoints (`/cast_spell` from T.1, `/cast_hunters_mark` and `/cast_hex` from C.2, `/use_cutting_words` from B.7, `/use_lay_on_hands` from priority #3) already populate `target_name` directly so no edit needed there.
**Description (cont 3):** What this DOESN'T do. Targeting doesn't yet drive damage application — `/attack` still returns damage_total + damage_breakdown for the rolling player to read; no HP is applied to the target's sheet automatically. That's **T.2 (auto-damage with campaign toggle)**, which the user wants next. T.8 is purely the visual feedback for what T.1 already wired into the broadcasts.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_lookup_combatant_name(campaign_id, combatant_id)` helper.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/attack` — `target_name` field on broadcast + response.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — `_targetTagHtml(d)` helper + integration into all three chat-card renderers.
- `app/templates/tabletop.html` — `.target-tag` CSS (crimson rounded pill).
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — `.atk-strike` toast note appends `→ TARGET`.
- `tests/harness/test_attack_buff_intercepts.py` — `test_attack_broadcast_includes_target_name`. Suite 128 → 129.

### Notes
- **What to test:** Open `/campaign/1` as Bob. Double-click Vex on the map → 🎯 chip appears. Open Thalindra's mini-sheet, click 🪄 Cast on Magic Missile → roll-log card now reads "🪄 Magic Missile → Vex". Same for 🗡 Strike on weapon attacks — toast shows "🎯 Dagger → Vex — attack" + the chat card shows the tag. Cast Hunter's Mark / Hex / Cutting Words / Lay on Hands / Bardic Inspiration → `feature_used` cards now show `→ NAME` next to the feature name. Press Escape to clear targeting; subsequent actions show no tag.
- **Backward compat.** Pure additive — broadcasts without `target_name` (older payloads or actions fired without a target) render no tag.
- **Magic Missile clarification.** Magic Missile RAW is three darts × 1d4+1 each — there's no single damage roll at cast time, so no roll toast fires for the cast itself. The chat card has buttons to roll each dart manually (or all three with a damage picker). T.2's auto-damage will apply the rolled damage to the target's HP; until then, players manually apply HP via the init-tracker HP input.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (T.2) hit determination + auto-damage with `Campaign.auto_apply_damage` toggle. (T.9) mobile target selector popup. (T.3) save-spell target list + auto/prompt resolution. (T.4) heal flows wired to target.

## [2.22.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase T.1 — target_combatant_id wired into the action endpoints + sheet handlers.** Connects the v2.21.0 targeting state (set by double-clicking a token on the map) to the action POSTs. `/cast_spell` joins `/attack` (already done in Phase B) in accepting `target_combatant_id` / `target_character_id` / `target_name` body fields; the resolved target rides on the `spell_cast` WS broadcast + the response so chat cards can name the target. Mini-sheet `.mini-strike-btn` + `.mini-cast-btn` handlers (in the tabletop context) read `window._targetingState.getTargets()[0]` directly. Full-sheet `.atk-strike` + `.sp-cast` handlers (in a separate page or iframe drawer) read a localStorage mirror that `_targeting._publish()` keeps in sync. With this commit, double-clicking a token on the map and then clicking Strike / Cast attaches the target to the action — no extra picker dialog. MINOR — new endpoint body field (`target_*` on `/cast_spell`) is additive; existing callers without the fields work as before.
**Description:** Four coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/cast_spell` — accepts three new optional body fields (`target_combatant_id`, `target_character_id`, `target_name`), parses them at the top of the handler, calls the existing `_resolve_target_combatant` helper (from Phase C.2's `/cast_hunters_mark`) to find the canonical combatant id + display name, and adds them to the `spell_cast` broadcast payload + the JSON response. When the caller passes an explicit `target_combatant_id` it wins over the char_id-resolved one — supports the "two tokens with the same name, pick this exact one" case. **(2)** `app/static/tabletop.js` `_targeting._publish()` — writes `JSON.stringify(getTargets())` to `localStorage` under `simplevtt:targeting:${CAMPAIGN_ID}` on every change, and `removeItem`s the key when the target list is empty. The key is per-campaign so two campaigns open in separate tabs don't cross-contaminate. **(3)** `app/templates/tabletop.html` `.mini-strike-btn` PC branch + `.mini-cast-btn` PC branch — read `window._targetingState.getTargets()[0]` (single target for now; multi-target lands with the T.5+ AoE pickers) and spread `target_combatant_id` / `target_character_id` / `target_name` into the fetch body. NPC mini-sheet branches unchanged (they use raw `/roll`, not the structured endpoints). **(4)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — new `_readSharedTarget()` + `_targetBodyFields()` helpers in the attacks IIFE that parse the localStorage mirror; `.atk-strike` POST body spreads the result. Same pattern inlined in the spells IIFE (separate scope, so the helper is duplicated locally rather than promoted to a window global). **(5)** `tests/harness/test_cast_spell_target.py` — 4 new tests: happy path with `target_character_id`, explicit `target_combatant_id` wins, no-target case, NPC-by-name resolution. Suite grows 124 → 128.
**Description (cont):** Why localStorage for cross-tab sync. The full character sheet runs at `/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/sheet` — a separate page from `/campaign/{cid}` (the tabletop). When opened in a new tab (player flow) or an iframe drawer (GM flow), it has its own `window` object — no direct access to `window._targetingState`. Three options were considered: (a) localStorage with synchronous read on click (chosen — simplest, no plumbing); (b) BroadcastChannel API with subscribed listeners (more modern but adds complexity for a 4-line read); (c) WS-broadcast targeting state (overkill — would publish every double-click to every connected player). LocalStorage's `setItem` is synchronous + immediately visible across same-origin tabs; a stale read is bounded to "old target from before the user double-clicked," which is the same staleness shape the rest of the multi-tab demo handles gracefully.
**Description (cont 2):** Why no `target_combatant_ids` list yet. Multi-target attacks (Magic Missile's 3 darts, Eldritch Blast's 2 beams at Lv 5+) need a list field, AND the per-target damage rolls need T.3 (save-spell flow) or T.5 (AoE) to fire. T.1's scope is single-target wiring; the existing Shift+double-click in v2.21.0 keeps adding entries to `_targetingState.tokenIds` but only the first is consumed by the action POST until those follow-ups land. The UI shows the multi-select rings so the player knows targets are queued; they'll matter once the multi-target endpoints ship.
**Description (cont 3):** What this enables in the live demo. Open `/campaign/1` as `demo-bob@example.com` (Thalindra). Double-click Vex on the map → 🎯 chip appears. Open Thalindra's mini-sheet, click Cast on Magic Missile → the chat card now shows "🪄 Thalindra casts Magic Missile at Vex" instead of "Thalindra casts Magic Missile." Same effect from the full sheet: double-click Vex, open Thalindra's full sheet (📋 Sheet), click Cast — target rides on the POST via the localStorage mirror. /attack already had target_combatant_id since Phase B (v2.20.0), so Strike on Krieger's Greataxe with Vex targeted will now correctly trigger Hunter's Mark / Hex damage riders when applicable (target match condition is on `combatant_id`).
**Description (cont 4):** Deferred follow-ups filed.
- **T.2: hit determination + auto-damage.** Server reads target.ac, computes hit/miss, auto-applies HP changes (gated by a new `Campaign.auto_apply_damage` setting defaulting to off). Includes undo button. Bigger commit.
- **T.3: save-DC spells with target list.** `/cast_spell` accepts `target_combatant_ids: [...]` (list) for AoE; server rolls saves for NPCs, prompts PCs.
- **T.4: heal flows.** `/use_lay_on_hands`, Cure Wounds, Healing Word auto-apply healing to the resolved target.
- **T.5–T.7: AoE pickers** (sphere, cone with rotation, line/cube/self) — board-based shape placement.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/cast_spell` — accepts `target_combatant_id` / `target_character_id` / `target_name`; resolves via `_resolve_target_combatant`; adds to `spell_cast` broadcast + response.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` `_targeting._publish()` — mirrors current targets to `localStorage[simplevtt:targeting:${CAMPAIGN_ID}]`.
- `app/templates/tabletop.html` `.mini-strike-btn` + `.mini-cast-btn` PC branches — spread current target descriptors into POST body.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — `_readSharedTarget()` + `_targetBodyFields()` helpers; wired into `.atk-strike` POST body; inline equivalent in `.sp-cast` POST body.
- `tests/harness/test_cast_spell_target.py` — 4 tests covering target_character_id, explicit combatant_id wins, no-target, target_name-only (NPC). Suite 124 → 128.

### Notes
- **What to test:** Open `/campaign/1` as Bob (`demo-bob@example.com`). Double-click Vex on the map → 🎯 chip appears with "Vex". Open Thalindra's mini-sheet, expand a Lv 1+ spell row, click 🪄 Cast → chat-card / roll log entry mentions "at Vex". Click 🗡 Strike on a Dagger row — same. Open Thalindra's full sheet (📋 Sheet button or right-click), click Cast on a spell — target still rides through (via localStorage). Clear target with Escape; subsequent Cast clicks show no target name.
- **Existing callers unchanged.** Any client that doesn't pass `target_*` body fields gets the prior behavior — empty target fields in the broadcast.
- **Multi-target / AoE deferred.** Shift+double-clicking still accumulates ring-decorated tokens but T.1 only consumes the first one. T.3 + T.5 light up the list field.
- **Full sheet via localStorage = same-origin only.** Cross-origin embedding wouldn't share the storage; the demo is always same-origin so this is fine.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (T.2) hit determination + auto-apply damage with campaign toggle + undo. (T.3) `target_combatant_ids` list on `/cast_spell` + save resolution. (T.4) heal flows to target. (T.5) AoE sphere picker. (T.6) cone with rotation. (T.7) line / cube / self-centered.

## [2.21.2] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **iPad Pro Magic Keyboard trackpad — right-click opens the character sheet.** The trackpad's two-finger-tap (or Control+click) on iPad Pro fires `pointerdown` with `button === 2` and `pointerType === 'mouse'`, but iOS Safari sometimes intercepts the subsequent `contextmenu` event (the system text-selection long-press menu wins). Adds a parallel `pointerdown` listener that filters to `button === 2` + non-touch pointer types so trackpad right-clicks open the sheet even when `contextmenu` doesn't fire. A 300 ms debounce flag prevents double-opening the sheet when both events fire for the same gesture (which happens on most desktop browsers + non-Safari iPad cases). PATCH — additive gesture coverage; no contract changes.
**Description:** One edit in `app/static/tabletop.js`. The existing `_handleRightClick` function gained a `_lastSheetOpenAt` timestamp + a 300 ms early-return guard. New `_handleRightClickPointer(ev)` wraps `_handleRightClick` with two filters: `ev.button !== 2` (so primary-button clicks don't open sheets) and `ev.pointerType === 'touch'` (so touchscreen taps don't fire this path — they go through the v2.21.0 touch double-tap path). The wrapper attaches to both `canvas` and `mapPane`, matching the contextmenu listener's defense-in-depth pattern. On standard desktop, both `pointerdown` (button:2) and `contextmenu` fire — the second is debounced. On iPad Pro Magic Keyboard trackpad, `pointerdown` fires reliably while `contextmenu` may be eaten by iOS — the new path catches it. On touchscreen, both filters skip — no regression to the existing touch double-tap = target gesture.

### Fixed
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — iPad Pro Magic Keyboard trackpad right-click now opens the character sheet via a `pointerdown` listener that fires even when iOS Safari suppresses the `contextmenu` event.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — 300 ms debounce flag prevents the sheet from opening twice when both `pointerdown` and `contextmenu` fire for the same gesture.

### Notes
- **What to test:** iPad Pro with Magic Keyboard: two-finger tap on a PC token (or NPC token) → character / monster sheet opens. Control+click on the trackpad does the same. Normal trackpad clicks (primary button) still drag/pan as expected — the new handler skips them via `button !== 2`. Desktop Chrome / Firefox / Safari unchanged (the existing `contextmenu` handler still fires; the `pointerdown` path is debounced out). Touch-only iPads (no trackpad) unchanged — the `pointerType === 'touch'` filter skips touchscreen taps so the v2.21.0 double-tap = target gesture is preserved.
- **Why a 300 ms debounce.** Desktop browsers fire BOTH `pointerdown` (button:2) AND `contextmenu` for a right-click — without the debounce, the sheet would open twice (one synthetic anchor click per event). 300 ms is conservative; the two events fire within ~1 ms in practice, so any value above ~20 ms would work.
- **Backward compat.** Pure additive. No new endpoints; no schema migration. Existing right-click behavior on desktop is unchanged.

---

## [2.21.1] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Fix-up for v2.21.0 T.0: right-click sheet-open works for NPCs too + uses the iframe drawer; long-press → openSheet removed from touch.** Two reported regressions in v2.21.0's targeting gestures: (1) right-click did nothing when the user clicked an NPC token (the handler filtered on `t.character_id` only, so monsters were skipped); (2) touch controls felt broken because the long-press timer fired `window.open` from a setTimeout callback — iOS Safari blocks popups from async non-user-gesture contexts AND the iOS native long-press-on-image menu competes for the same gesture, so the touch long-press was unreliable in two distinct ways. This commit rewires right-click to open both character + monster sheets via a synthetic `a.character-sheet-link` / `a.monster-sheet-link` anchor click — the existing GM iframe-drawer interceptor picks it up automatically, side-stepping popup blockers entirely. Adds a parallel `contextmenu` listener on `mapPane` (defense in depth — fires even if an overlay sits between cursor and canvas). Removes the touch long-press timer + its supporting touchmove cancel logic + the `_longPressFired` branch in touchend. Touch sheet-open now falls back to the init-tracker "📋 Sheet" button until a better touch gesture lands. PATCH — bug-fix release; no contract changes.
**Description:** Three edits in `app/static/tabletop.js`. **(1)** Right-click handler: factored into `_openSheetForToken(token)` + `_handleRightClick(ev)`. The token resolver tries `character_id` first then falls back to `token_template_id` (NPC monster sheet path). Builds a hidden `<a>` element with the right class, appends to body, calls `.click()`, removes — the v2.3.15 + v2.3.33 document-level interceptor (in `tabletop.html`) catches the click and opens the URL in the GM's iframe drawer. For non-GMs (no interceptor wrapped under `{% if is_gm %}`) the anchor's `target="_blank"` falls through to a normal new tab, which the browser allows because the click originated from a user gesture. Either way the popup-blocker bypass via `window.open` from JS is avoided. The listener attaches to both `canvas` and `mapPane`, so any overlay z-index issue between cursor and canvas still resolves correctly. **(2)** Touch `touchstart` long-press timer + its `setTimeout` block removed entirely. Replaced with a comment block explaining the two failure modes (popup-blocking + iOS native long-press collision). **(3)** Touch `touchmove` handler: removed the long-press-cancel block (the timer no longer exists). Touch `touchend`: removed the `_longPressFired` branch since the flag never gets set. The double-tap target-pick logic stays intact.
**Description (cont):** Why the iframe-drawer approach (not `window.open`). The v2.3.15 monster-sheet drawer + v2.3.33 PC-sheet drawer were both designed around a delegated document click handler that intercepts `a.character-sheet-link` / `a.monster-sheet-link` clicks and loads the URL into a side iframe. The init-tracker's 📋 Sheet button already uses this pattern. Right-clicking a token now goes through the same pipeline — consistent UX, no popup blockers, no new infrastructure. The trade-off is that non-GM players see a new browser tab (the interceptor is GM-gated); when player-side sheet UX wants the drawer too, the existing `{% if is_gm %}` gate can be relaxed in a separate commit.
**Description (cont 2):** Why drop touch long-press without a replacement. The combination of popup-blocker behavior + iOS's native long-press image menu means a setTimeout-driven long-press fundamentally can't open a new tab reliably on touch. Alternatives that DO work include: (a) two-finger tap → openSheet (collides with the existing pinch-zoom gesture), (b) long-press-on-EMPTY-MAP → enter a "tap a token to open" mode (extra step, more state), (c) a permanent on-screen "Sheet" toggle button visible in touch mode. Each has friction. Rather than ship a half-working gesture, this commit removes touch sheet-open and leaves the init-tracker "📋 Sheet" button as the discoverable path. A future commit can revisit with option (b) or (c).

### Fixed
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — right-click on NPC tokens now opens the monster sheet (was skipped — only PC tokens with `character_id` were handled).
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — right-click sheet-open uses synthetic anchor click that flows through the v2.3.15 iframe-drawer interceptor for GMs; sidesteps popup blockers that silently swallowed `window.open` in some browser configs.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — parallel `contextmenu` listener on `mapPane` so overlay z-index issues don't suppress the handler.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — removed buggy touch long-press → openSheet timer (popup-blocked + competes with iOS native long-press menu).

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Right-click any PC token → character sheet opens in the GM iframe drawer (or a new tab for non-GMs). Right-click any NPC token (Vex, a bandit, the goblin captain) → monster sheet opens the same way. On iPad, touch sheet-open via the init-tracker 📋 Sheet button (long-press was removed). All other test cases from v2.21.0 (double-click target, Shift multi-target, Escape clear, ring rendering, chip UI) continue to work.
- **Backward compat.** Pure bug-fix; no contract changes.
- **Filed for future commit.** Touch gesture for sheet-open — two-finger tap, long-press-on-empty-map, or a permanent on-screen toggle button. v2.21.1 leaves this unhandled.

---

## [2.21.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase T.0 — board targeting state + gesture remap + ring overlay.** First commit in the targeting-system roadmap. Players double-click a token on the map to designate it as the target of their next attack / spell / heal / check; Shift+double-click adds more targets (Magic Missile / Eldritch Blast multi-target). A crimson glowing ring appears on each targeted token; a floating "🎯 Targeting: NAME ×" chip anchors at the bottom-center of the map pane and disappears when no target is set. Touch double-tap mirrors the mouse double-click. Escape clears all targets. The character-sheet gesture migrated off double-click — right-click (desktop) or long-press ~600ms (touch) now opens the sheet. The state lives on `window._targetingState` for the T.1 sheet-handler wire-up; this commit is the visual + interaction layer only. MINOR — new visible UI surface + new gesture semantics; no server contract changes.
**Description:** Three file edits. **(1)** `app/static/tabletop.js` — new `_targeting` state module right before `_drawRing`. Public surface: `setTarget(tokenId)`, `addTarget(tokenId)` (multi), `toggleTarget(tokenId)`, `clear()`, `isTargeted(tokenId)`, `getTargets()` (resolves token IDs to richer `{token_id, character_id, token_template_id, combatant_id, name, color}` descriptors so the T.1 sheet handlers can pick the right selector for each endpoint). Exposed via `window._targetingState`. `_publish()` redraws the canvas + updates the chip UI + dispatches a `vtt:targeting-change` CustomEvent for any future listeners. New helper `_updateTargetingChip()` creates / updates / removes a `<div id="targeting-chip">` in `#map-pane`; chip carries one × button calling `_targeting.clear()`. New helper `_maybeShowTargetingHint()` shows a one-time onboarding toast the first time targeting fires (gate via `localStorage['vtt:targeting-hint-shown']`). **(2)** `app/static/tabletop.js` `render()` — after the existing token + spawn + breadcrumb draw passes, walks every token and draws a crimson `r+4` ring with `shadowBlur: 10` on tokens that are in `_targeting.tokenIds`. Skips hidden tokens for non-GM viewers. **(3)** `app/static/tabletop.js` gesture handlers: `dblclick` now calls `_targeting.setTarget(t.id)` (or `addTarget` when Shift is held) instead of `openSheet`. `contextmenu` now opens the sheet instead of just `preventDefault`. New `keydown` listener clears the target on Escape unless an input / textarea / contentEditable is focused (so the dice-expression input keeps owning its own Escape). Touch `touchstart` arms a 600 ms long-press timer that opens the sheet if no movement happens; `touchmove` aborts the timer when the finger moves > 12 px; double-tap (existing detection) calls `_targeting.setTarget` instead of `openSheet`. The long-press uses a `_longPressFired` flag on the `tapStart` object so the subsequent touchend doesn't ALSO register a tap. **(4)** `app/templates/tabletop.html` — new `.targeting-chip` CSS block: absolutely positioned at `bottom: 16px; left: 50%; transform: translateX(-50%)` inside `#map-pane` (which already has `position: relative`); crimson palette matching the in-canvas ring; backdrop-filter blur; 44 px × end button per the CLAUDE.md touch-target rule (this is sheet-context UI, not the dense init-tracker exception).
**Description (cont):** Why double-click was the right gesture to steal. Per the v2.21.0 plan-doc questionnaire (option A), double-click maps 1:1 to "I'm picking this thing" in every modern VTT (Roll20, Foundry, Owlbear) and feels native to combat targeting where the player frequently swaps targets mid-turn. Right-click + long-press is the established replacement for sheet-open across the same VTTs. The init-tracker "📋 Sheet" button stays as the discoverable fallback for first-time users who don't yet know the new gestures.
**Description (cont 2):** Why client-side ephemeral state (not server-broadcast). Targeting is per-player and changes constantly — broadcasting every double-click would flood the WS hub. The GM intent broadcast (option for "Alice is targeting Vex") is filed for a future opt-in campaign setting; today the target rides on the action POST (attack / cast / heal) so the GM sees the target name in the chat card when the action fires, not before. Trade-off: GM can't peek at a player's planned target before they roll. Acceptable for v1; revisit if play-testing reveals friction.
**Description (cont 3):** Why a one-time onboarding hint. Existing demo users have muscle memory: double-click = sheet. The first time they double-click after the upgrade, they'll target a token by accident. The hint toast ("🎯 Target set. Right-click — or long-press on touch — opens the character sheet.") fires once per browser via localStorage; subsequent targets stay silent.
**Description (cont 4):** What this unlocks for T.1+. The sheet's `.atk-strike` / `.sp-cast` / `.cf-use` / `.inv-use` click handlers will read `window._targetingState.getTargets()` and attach `target_combatant_id` / `target_character_id` / `target_name` (whichever is set) to the POST body. Endpoints that already accept those fields (Phase B's `/attack`, v2.10.0's `/use_lay_on_hands`, v2.11.0's `/use_bardic_inspiration`, v2.15.7's `/use_cutting_words`, v2.19.1's `/cast_hunters_mark` + `/cast_hex`) immediately benefit. `/cast_spell` will grow a `target_combatant_ids` list field in T.1. T.2 then teaches `/attack` to read `target.ac` and compute hit/miss; T.3 wires save-spell targets; T.4 wires heal flows; T.5–T.7 add AoE shape pickers (sphere → cone → line/cube/self).

### Added
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — `_targeting` state module + `window._targetingState` global + `_updateTargetingChip()` + `_maybeShowTargetingHint()`.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — `render()` draws crimson target rings on every targeted token (hidden tokens skipped for non-GM).
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — `dblclick` sets target (Shift = add); `contextmenu` opens character sheet; `keydown(Escape)` clears.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` — touch double-tap sets target; 600 ms long-press opens character sheet.
- `app/templates/tabletop.html` — `.targeting-chip` CSS (floating bottom-center chip + 44 px × button).

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Double-click any token on the map — a crimson ring appears around it + the "🎯 Targeting: NAME ×" chip floats at the bottom of the map. First time → onboarding toast about the new right-click gesture. Press Escape → chip disappears + ring fades. Click × on the chip → same. Shift+double-click a second token → both rings visible + chip shows "Vex, Bandit Alpha". Right-click any PC token → character sheet opens in a new tab. On iPad: double-tap a token = target; long-press ~0.6 s = sheet opens.
- **Existing keybindings / handlers unchanged.** Single-click still does nothing (selection-vs-drag pattern preserved). Token drag still works exactly as before. The dice-roll expression input still owns Escape (the keydown listener checks `activeElement.tagName`).
- **NPC targeting** is currently silent — no broadcast to the GM about who you're targeting. Filed for a future opt-in campaign setting (`targeting_intent`).
- **Hidden tokens** (GM-only `is_hidden=true`) can't be targeted by non-GM players — the dblclick / dbltap handlers + the ring-draw all skip them. GM can target hidden tokens freely.
- **Backward compat.** No server contract changes. Reverting this commit returns the demo to v2.20.0's gesture set without data migration.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (T.1) target_combatant_id wired into every action endpoint + sheet handler. (T.2) /attack reads target AC, computes hit/miss, auto-applies damage on hit (gated by new Campaign.auto_apply_damage setting, default off). (T.3) save spells with target list + NPC auto-save / PC prompt. (T.4) heal flows wired to target. (T.5) AoE sphere/circle picker on the canvas. (T.6) AoE cone with mouse rotation + Shift snap. (T.7) line / cube / self-centered sphere. (T.8) chat-card per-target rendering.

## [2.20.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B — damage-flow roll-time intercepts.** /attack now reads the attacker's buffs from the hub battle state + class features from the sheet and auto-applies four kinds of uplifts at damage-roll time: Rage flat damage bonus + advantage on STR attacks (Krieger); Hunter's Mark / Hex per-hit dice when the marked target matches (Rowan / Magnus); Colossus Slayer +1d6 vs below-max-HP target, once per turn (Rowan). /sheet-fields takes a new `damage_type` body field and halves the damage when the target has a buff with matching `resistance_to` — Rage's resistance to physical lands automatically. Each auto-uplift is a separate dice roll; the response carries a structured `auto_uplifts` list with per-entry `{label, expression, total, breakdown, damage_type, source}` plus an aggregate `auto_uplift_total` already folded into `damage_total`. New `target_combatant_id` body field on /attack threads the target through for buff-matching + below-max-HP checks. MINOR — additive contract changes, no schema migration; existing /attack callers that don't pass `target_combatant_id` still work (target-keyed uplifts simply don't fire).
**Description:** Six coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new module helpers near `_rage_damage_bonus`: `_PHYSICAL_DAMAGE_TYPES` constant, `_hunter_level_from_sheet`, `_compute_attack_auto_uplifts`, `_mark_colossus_slayer_used`, `_has_rage_str_advantage`, `_resistance_halve`. `_compute_attack_auto_uplifts` returns a structured list of uplifts after reading the attacker's combatant from the hub state + its `buffs` list + the sheet's `class_features` list. Each uplift carries a label, dice expression, rolled total, breakdown string, damage_type, and source slug. **(2)** `use_attack` endpoint extended: accepts `target_combatant_id`; reads `_has_rage_str_advantage` BEFORE the d20 roll and stamps `2d20kh1` when Rage advantage applies (skipped when the character's roll_state already set advantage/disadvantage — the existing `_apply_roll_state` wins to avoid double-stacking); after the base damage roll, calls `_compute_attack_auto_uplifts` and folds the aggregate total into `damage_total`; marks Colossus Slayer "used this turn" via `_mark_colossus_slayer_used` when the uplift fired. The `auto_uplifts` list + `auto_uplift_total` + `target_combatant_id` are added to both the weapon_attack broadcast payload and the JSON response so the rolling player's chat-toast can render the breakdown without waiting for the WS round-trip. **(3)** `patch_sheet_fields` extended: reads `damage_type` body field; when `is_damage` is True AND `damage_amount > 0`, calls `_resistance_halve(damage_amount, damage_type, char.sheet)` which scans the target's `_buffs_active` for any buff whose `effects.resistance_to` contains the damage type and halves the damage (floor). The halved value flows into `_apply_hp_change` so the death-save state machine + massive-damage threshold both see the post-resistance number (RAW correct — resistance applies before HP is dealt). Response adds `resistance_applied` + `damage_amount_after_resistance` so the player UI can surface a "🛡 resisted" toast. **(4)** `app/templates/tabletop.html` — GM-side turn-advance handler resets `combatant.economy.colossus_slayer_used = false` alongside the action/bonus/reaction chips when a combatant's turn begins. Both the per-turn reset AND the "Start initiative" full reset paths get the new line. **(5)** `tests/harness/test_attack_buff_intercepts.py` — 9 new tests: Rage damage +2 on Greataxe, Rage advantage = `2d20kh1` in breakdown, Hunter's Mark fires vs marked target, Hunter's Mark skips other targets, Colossus Slayer vs below-max-HP target, Colossus Slayer skipped at max HP, Colossus Slayer once per turn, resistance halves slashing on raging target, resistance doesn't halve mismatched type. Suite grows 115 → 124.
**Description (cont):** Why aggregate `auto_uplifts` into `damage_total`. The existing weapon_attack chat card renders one "Damage" line built from `damage_total`. Folding the aggregate keeps that line accurate without forcing every chat-card consumer to learn the new `auto_uplifts` shape; the structured list is exposed alongside for future per-type display work (rendering each uplift on its own labeled line with the right damage_type icon). Cost: a chat card today reads a single total that mixes weapon damage with Rage / Hunter's Mark / etc. — acceptable since the breakdown string includes a one-line summary ("1d12+4=10 + Rage +2 + Hunter's Mark 1d6=4") that explains the components. A future commit can split the rendering to show each uplift as its own line.
**Description (cont 2):** Why a heuristic for "STR-based attack" (physical damage type). RAW: Rage advantage applies to attacks where the attack roll uses STR; Rage damage bonus applies to melee weapon attacks made with STR. The attack-row schema doesn't carry an explicit `ability: "STR"` field — players write the attack name + bonus + damage. Rather than infer ability from attack-bonus arithmetic against the character's STR mod (fragile), use damage_type ∈ {bludgeoning, piercing, slashing} as a proxy. A barbarian with a finesse rapier WOULD get Rage benefits via this rule even though RAW says they don't (DEX-based finesse). Demo Barbarian (Krieger) only has Greataxe + Javelin, both STR-based, so the rule is correct in practice. A future commit can tighten to "STR + physical" if a finesse barbarian fixture lands.
**Description (cont 3):** Why server-rolled advantage (not via the existing `_apply_roll_state`). `_apply_roll_state` exists for character-level adv/dis (set via the roll-state pill or buff cards in v2.2.0). Layering Rage advantage on top by mutating the expression keeps the change scoped to /attack — no global side effect, no risk of contaminating /roll, /save, etc. The replacement `1d20 → 2d20kh1` is conditional on neither `kh1` nor `kl1` already being in the expression so character-level disadvantage continues to win (advantage + disadvantage = even per RAW, modeled today as "whichever roll-state was set first"). roll_state_applied gets the string "advantage_rage" so the client can show why advantage applied.
**Description (cont 4):** Why resistance only halves in /sheet-fields (not in /attack). Damage application happens after the GM/player decides whether the attack hit — /attack just rolls dice. Resistance applies when damage is actually taken, which is when /sheet-fields receives the HP change with `hp_change_reason: damage`. The existing damage flow (init-tracker HP edit + sheet save) already routes through this endpoint, so resistance fires correctly. A future "click on chat card to apply damage" flow would need to PATCH /sheet-fields with `damage_type` for resistance to apply.
**Description (cont 5):** Deferred follow-ups (filed as Phase B.1+).
- **Reckless Attack** (Barbarian Lv 2): /use_reckless_attack endpoint installs a "reckless" buff with `effects.advantage_on: [str_melee_attack]` AND `incoming_attacks_have_advantage: True` for 1 round. /attack reads the first; future incoming-attack resolution reads the second.
- **Improved Critical** (Champion Lv 3): crit on 19-20. /attack reads attacker class_features, lowers crit threshold from 20. Garrik's class_features needs the entry added.
- **Brutal Critical** (Barbarian Lv 9+): extra crit dice. Krieger is Lv 5 so not eligible yet; ship when a Lv 9+ Barbarian fixture lands.
- **Dark One's Blessing** (Fiend Warlock): temp HP when attacker reduces target to 0. /attack would need to inspect the target's hp_current + the rolled damage to detect the kill; the buff target hasn't been damage-applied yet at /attack time. Easier path: add a "Dark One's Blessing eligible — click to apply" button on the chat card when target_combatant_id is set + Magnus has the feature + damage_total > target.hp_current.
- **Sharpshooter / Great Weapon Master** (-5 atk / +10 dmg trade): extends the v2.16.0 per-attack uplift picker with feat-driven options. Player opts in pre-roll.
- **Stunning Strike** (Monk Lv 5): per-attack Ki uplift in the v2.16.0 picker. Similar shape to Divine Smite (spend resource + on-hit save).

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_compute_attack_auto_uplifts(...)`, `_mark_colossus_slayer_used(...)`, `_has_rage_str_advantage(...)`, `_resistance_halve(...)`, `_hunter_level_from_sheet(...)`, `_PHYSICAL_DAMAGE_TYPES`.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `/attack` accepts `target_combatant_id`; applies Rage advantage, computes auto_uplifts, marks Colossus Slayer "used this turn", returns + broadcasts the structured list.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `/sheet-fields` accepts `damage_type`; applies target resistance via `_resistance_halve`; response carries `resistance_applied` + `damage_amount_after_resistance`.
- `app/templates/tabletop.html` — GM `nextTurn` + `battle-start-btn` reset `economy.colossus_slayer_used` alongside the action chips.
- `tests/harness/test_attack_buff_intercepts.py` — 9 tests covering all five uplift paths + resistance halving + once-per-turn gating. Suite 115 → 124.

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open Krieger's mini-sheet, click Rage. Click Greataxe — chat card shows attack with `2d20kh1` (advantage) + damage `1d12+4 + Rage +2`. Rowan: cast Hunter's Mark on Pip via dev console (`fetch('/api/campaign/1/cast_hunters_mark', {...})`), then click Longbow with `target_combatant_id: pip_token_id` — chat card shows base + Hunter's Mark +1d6. If Pip is below max HP, Colossus Slayer +1d6 also fires. Repeat — Colossus Slayer doesn't fire (used this turn). GM clicks "Next turn" past Rowan — flag resets. Krieger takes 10 slashing damage via init-tracker HP edit (sets `hp_change_reason=damage` + `damage_amount=10` + `damage_type=slashing`) — toast shows "🛡 Resistance applied (5 dealt)"; HP drops by 5 not 10.
- **Existing /attack callers unchanged.** Callers that don't pass `target_combatant_id` see no Hunter's Mark / Hex / Colossus Slayer uplifts (target-keyed uplifts need the target). Rage and resistance work without it.
- **Roll log impact.** The weapon_attack chat card still renders a single damage line. The new `auto_uplifts` list rides on the payload for a future commit to split into per-uplift labeled lines.
- **Phase A + C are complete; Phase B has its first slice.** With this commit Rage / Hunter's Mark / Hex / Colossus Slayer all auto-apply at the right roll time, and resistance auto-halves. The five deferred B.x items (Reckless Attack, Improved Critical, Brutal Critical, Dark One's Blessing, Sharpshooter) are filed in the "What this unlocks" notes above for follow-up commits.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.2) `/use_reckless_attack` installs reckless buff. (B.3) Improved Critical 19-20 crit threshold for Champion. (B.4) Dark One's Blessing trigger on damage card. (B.5) Sharpshooter / Great Weapon Master in the v2.16.0 picker. (B.6) Stunning Strike Ki uplift for Kael. (UI polish) Chat-card split rendering for auto_uplifts.

## [2.19.2] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase C.3 — sheet-side buff descriptive layer.** Wraps Phase C. Each combatant's buff list now mirrors onto the character sheet's `_buffs_active` JSON field; the full sheet renders an "Active Effects" fieldset (purple chips with × end button + concentration ⏱ marker + tooltip) that lives between Defenses and Class Resources. The mirror updates on every install path (/use_rage, /cast_hunters_mark, /cast_hex), every remove path (/end_buff, concentration save fail), and PUT /battle (catches the GM's client-side auto-expire ticks). `duration_rounds` + `duration_max` are stripped from the sheet copy — the init tracker stays the source of truth for live countdowns; the sheet just shows presence + effects. New GET /api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/buffs read endpoint returns both the live hub view + the sheet mirror (mostly for harness verification). MINOR — new visible sheet UI + new persistent sheet field + new GET endpoint. **Phase C is complete:** buff infrastructure spans hub state (C.1) → concentration semantics (C.2) → persistent sheet layer (C.3). Next is **Phase B** — the (B) roll-time intercept reads `combatant.buffs[].effects` to apply Rage damage, Hunter's Mark / Hex riders, etc.
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new `_mirror_buffs_to_sheet(db, character_id, buffs)` helper. Strips `duration_rounds` and `duration_max`, writes to `char.sheet["_buffs_active"]`, flags modified, commits. Idempotent — early-returns when the sheet field already matches the new list. **(2)** Five install/remove call sites updated to call the mirror helper after the hub mutation: /use_rage (after `_install_buff`), /cast_hunters_mark (after install), /cast_hex (after install), /end_buff (after `_remove_buff`), `_maybe_concentration_save` (the fail path, db threaded through as a new optional kwarg). **(3)** PUT /api/campaign/{cid}/battle handler — after `hub.set_battle`, walks every combatant with a `char_id`, calls `_mirror_buffs_to_sheet` to sync the sheet to the new buff list. This catches the GM's client-side auto-expire ticks: when the GM hits "Next turn", the JS decrements `duration_rounds` locally for buffs the ending combatant owns + drops 0-rounds entries + pushes the whole state via PUT /battle. The server's mirror walk picks up the changes for each PC combatant (NPCs skipped — they have no sheet). **(4)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — new "Active Effects" fieldset between Defenses and Class Resources. Hidden `<textarea id="active-effects-data">` carries the server-rendered JSON snapshot of `sheet["_buffs_active"]` for initial population. JS IIFE renders one `.ae-chip` per buff (icon + name + target name if any + ⏱ Conc marker for concentration buffs + tooltip with full desc) plus a 44 px × `.ae-end` button. WS `buff_update` listener patches the local list (stripped to match the mirror) + re-renders. `.ae-end` click POSTs `/end_buff` (optimistic local removal + WS confirmation). CSS for `.ae-chip` + `.ae-end` matches the init-tracker's purple-tinted `.buff-chip` styling so a player recognises the same chip in both places. **(5)** New GET `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/buffs` endpoint — returns `{character_id, buffs (live hub view), sheet_buffs (mirror)}`. Primary use case is harness verification that the mirror is in sync; secondary use case is a fallback hydration path for the sheet if the inlined textarea is missing (older saves / unsaved state). **(6)** `tests/harness/test_buff_sheet_mirror.py` — 5 new tests: /use_rage mirrors with duration stripped + effects preserved, /end_buff clears the mirror, /cast_hunters_mark mirrors with target info preserved, PUT /battle with a tick'd buff list mirrors, PUT /battle with an empty list clears the mirror. Suite grows 110 → 115.
**Description (cont):** Why strip duration_rounds + duration_max from the sheet mirror. Two reasons: (a) the init tracker is the live countdown — it auto-decrements on every GM turn-advance, and showing two competing duration displays would confuse players when they desync briefly; (b) the sheet is rendered on page load and stays static until a WS event fires, so a stored `duration_rounds: 7` could look fresh when it's actually 7 rounds old. Stripping forces the chip text to be "presence only" and avoids the staleness gap. The effects field (damage bonus, advantage_on, resistance_to, weapon_hit_bonus_dice, etc.) is preserved — those don't decay during the buff's lifetime, so they're safe to mirror.
**Description (cont 2):** Why PUT /battle is the auto-expire sync point. The GM's `nextTurn` handler in `tabletop.html` ticks each buff's `duration_rounds` client-side (the GM is authoritative for battle state), drops expired entries, then pushes the whole state via PUT /battle. The server's PUT handler now also re-mirrors each PC's buff list to their sheet — costs one DB query + at most one write per PC combatant per turn-end. For a 12-PC demo encounter that's 12 queries per turn-advance; the mirror helper is idempotent (no-op writes when nothing changed) so the actual write count is usually 0-2. Acceptable. The alternative (server-side auto-expire timer) would duplicate the GM's authority over turn timing and complicate the data flow for marginal benefit.
**Description (cont 3):** Why a sheet-only data field instead of always reading from the hub. The hub battle state is in-memory only — if the app restarts, the buff list is lost. The sheet mirror persists in Postgres alongside the rest of the character sheet, so after an app restart a player loading their sheet still sees "🦬 Rage" + "🎯 Hunter's Mark → Vex". Once the GM reseats the encounter (Load encounter button hydrates the hub from the sheet's `_buffs_active` field — that hydration path can be a future C.4 commit if needed; today the player just sees the chips on their sheet and the GM can manually re-install them in the init tracker). Combined cost: one extra JSON field per character (typically 0-3 dicts).
**Description (cont 4):** What this unlocks. Phase B (roll-time intercept) can now ship cleanly:
- /attack reads attacker's `combatant.buffs` (from hub) for `melee_str_damage_bonus` (Rage), `weapon_hit_bonus_dice` (Hunter's Mark / Hex against target). For target-keyed riders, check `buff.effects.weapon_hit_bonus_target_combatant_id` against the attack's target combatant id.
- Save rolls intercept reads `advantage_on` and `resistance_to` fields.
- The sheet's full Active Effects panel gives the player visibility into what's active when they pick which spell/attack to fire — no need to flip back to the init tracker.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_mirror_buffs_to_sheet(db, character_id, buffs)` helper.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — five call sites mirror after install/remove (`/use_rage`, `/cast_hunters_mark`, `/cast_hex`, `/end_buff`, `_maybe_concentration_save` fail path).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — PUT `/api/campaign/{cid}/battle` mirrors every PC combatant's buff list to their sheet (catches GM auto-expire).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — GET `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/buffs` returns both live hub view + sheet mirror.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — new "Active Effects" fieldset between Defenses + Class Resources. Hidden `<textarea id="active-effects-data">` carries the Jinja-inlined `_buffs_active` snapshot. JS IIFE renders chips with × end + ⏱ Conc marker + tooltip + WS `buff_update` patching.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — `.ae-chip` + `.ae-end` CSS (44 px touch target for sheet context, mirrors the init-tracker purple palette).
- `tests/harness/test_buff_sheet_mirror.py` — 5 new tests covering install/remove/PUT-battle mirror paths + duration-stripping verification. Suite 110 → 115.

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open Krieger's full sheet (📋 Sheet) — initially no "Active Effects" section (auto-hidden when empty). Activate Rage from the mini-sheet — refresh the full sheet → "Active Effects" panel appears with a 🦬 Rage chip + × button + tooltip showing "+2 dmg, advantage on STR, resistance to physical." Click × → buff disappears + chip gone. Similar flow with Rowan's Hunter's Mark + Magnus's Hex (still requires manual `fetch()` from dev console to install since the .sp-cast intercept ships in a follow-up — see C.2 notes).
- **The sheet mirror is best-effort for currently-loaded characters.** If a player opens their sheet while combat is paused and a buff is installed mid-session, they need to refresh once to see the new chip (the textarea was rendered with the old state). Subsequent buff_update broadcasts patch in real-time without a refresh.
- **Backward compat.** Characters without `_buffs_active` on their sheet render the empty list (Jinja's `or []` fallback). No migration needed.
- **Phase C is complete.** Three commits: C.1 (data primitive + Rage + auto-expire), C.2 (concentration + Hunter's Mark + Hex + concentration save on damage), C.3 (sheet-side descriptive layer + Active Effects panel). Next: **Phase B (roll-time intercept)** — read `combatant.buffs[].effects` at `/attack`, `/cast_spell`, and save-roll time to apply Rage damage bonus, Hunter's Mark / Hex weapon-hit riders, etc.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.14) /attack reads Rage's `melee_str_damage_bonus`. (B.15) /attack reads Hunter's Mark / Hex's `weapon_hit_bonus_dice` and applies on hits against the marked target. (B.16) Sneak Attack / Divine Smite already exist (v2.16.0); extend the per-attack uplift picker to also offer Stunning Strike + Sharpshooter + Great Weapon Master. (C.2.1) Sheet-side .sp-cast intercept for hunters-mark / hex with target picker — UX polish that completes the Phase C demo flow.

## [2.19.1] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase C.2 — concentration + Hunter's Mark + Hex + concentration save on damage.** Extends the v2.19.0 buff slot with the RAW concentration semantics: at most one concentration buff per combatant (installing a second concentration buff drops the first); damage events automatically roll a CON save (DC max(10, damage//2)) and drop the buff on fail. Ships two new endpoints — `/cast_hunters_mark` (Ranger L1+, +1d6 weapon damage on hits against the marked target, concentration up to 1h/8h/24h depending on slot level) and `/cast_hex` (Warlock L1+, +1d6 necrotic on hits + disadvantage on a chosen ability, same scaling). Both endpoints atomically decrement a class-keyed spell slot + install a structured concentration buff (with effects fields the (B) Phase B intercept will read) + mark the bonus chip. Auto-rolled concentration saves broadcast `concentration_save` with the rolled value, DC, and pass/fail; a fail also fires a `buff_update` removing the buff. UI wiring (intercepting the sheet's spell row click for hunters-mark / hex slugs to open a target picker before firing the dedicated endpoint) ships in a follow-up commit so this one stays focused on the contract. MINOR — new endpoints + new concentration semantics + new `concentration_save` WS broadcast type.
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_install_buff` extended with concentration swap: when `buff["concentration"]` is truthy and the combatant already has another concentration buff, that one is dropped before the new one is installed (RAW "you lose concentration on the previous spell"). The broadcast's `buff_update` includes a new `replaced_concentration` list so the client can show a swap toast. **(2)** New `_concentration_buff_for(campaign_id, character_id)` read helper — returns the single concentration buff active on a combatant (or None) by scanning the buff list. **(3)** New `_maybe_concentration_save(campaign_id, char, damage_amount)` async helper — when called with damage_amount > 0 on a character with a concentration buff, rolls 1d20 + CON_mod + (PB if CON-save proficient — Barbarian / Fighter / Sorcerer per RAW; Krieger qualifies via his Barbarian saves, Garrik via Fighter, Zara via Sorcerer; others get CON_mod only). DC = max(10, damage_amount // 2). On fail calls `_remove_buff` (which fires its own buff_update broadcast); always broadcasts a `concentration_save` event with the rolled die, total, DC, pass/fail. **(4)** `patch_sheet_fields` endpoint (the only is_damage=True caller) — calls `_maybe_concentration_save(...)` after `_apply_hp_change` when reason=damage AND damage_amount > 0. **(5)** New `/api/campaign/{cid}/cast_hunters_mark` endpoint — validates Ranger class + Hunter's Mark on spell list (409 wrong-class / unavailable), Phase 4 over-budget gate on bonus slot, L1+ Ranger slot available (409 no_slot), decrements the slot + installs the Hunter's Mark concentration buff (target resolved via _resolve_target_combatant helper from target_character_id or target_name) + marks bonus chip. Duration scales with slot level (L1-L2 = 1h displayed, L3-L4 = 8h, L5+ = 24h; underlying duration_rounds capped at 100 for chip readability — RAW 1h = 600 rounds but the chip text wants to stay compact). **(6)** New `/api/campaign/{cid}/cast_hex` endpoint — same shape as Hunter's Mark but Warlock-keyed; finds the lowest available Pact slot ≥ requested level (Warlock Lv 5 has only L3 slots, so even a "L1 cast" upgrades to L3); accepts `ability` body field (one of STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA, defaults to STR) for the disadvantage rider. **(7)** New `_resolve_target_combatant(campaign_id, target_character_id, target_name)` helper — scans hub.combatants to find a stable combatant id matching either selector; returns (combatant_id, display_name) for the buff's target fields. **(8)** `tests/harness/test_concentration_buffs.py` — 8 new tests: Hunter's Mark happy path with buff install + broadcasts, wrong-class (Pip), missing target, missing character_id, Hex happy path with L3 slot + STR ability, Hex wrong-class, concentration swap (second cast replaces first), concentration save on damage end-to-end (install Hunter's Mark + PATCH /sheet-fields with damage + assert concentration_save broadcast). Suite grows 102 → 110.
**Description (cont):** Why concentration save is auto-rolled. Two alternatives considered: (a) auto-roll server-side (chosen) — simplest UX, no extra click required, mirrors how /attack rolls its die server-side then broadcasts the result; (b) emit a `concentration_save_required` event and let the player click a "Roll save" button in the toast, which then POSTs back. Rejected (b) because the existing concentration-flow has no client-side dice roller for arbitrary +CON saves outside the dice-roll panel — building one feels like Phase B work, not Phase C. Players who want to hand-roll can disable Hunter's Mark on their sheet and re-cast after each hit, or the GM can manually rebroadcast. The save uses CON + (PB if proficient); War Caster / Resilient (CON) feats that grant CON save proficiency aren't on any demo PC's sheet today, so the proficiency-checker is "saving_throws.CON" only.
**Description (cont 2):** Why the duration_rounds cap. RAW Hunter's Mark at L1 is "concentration, up to 1 hour" = 600 rounds. The init-tracker chip text wants to stay compact ("🎯 1h" / "🎯 8h" / "🎯 24h") so the chip displays a label string instead of a literal countdown. Internally the buff's duration_rounds is capped at 100 so the GM's turn-advance tick doesn't have to count down 600 turns. v1 deviation — concentration buffs effectively last as long as the encounter (or until ended manually / dropped on concentration-save fail) since 100 rounds = 10 minutes of in-game time and most encounters are 3-10 rounds. A future commit can lift the cap if anyone runs a 30-minute encounter.
**Description (cont 3):** Why Hunter's Mark and Hex don't share a single endpoint. They're nearly identical in shape — both bonus-action concentration spells with weapon-hit riders on a marked target. A combined `/cast_concentration_spell` endpoint could route by `spell_slug`. Rejected for v1 because: (a) class-keyed slot validation differs (Ranger vs Warlock); (b) Hex carries an `ability` field for the disadvantage rider that Hunter's Mark doesn't; (c) future concentration spells (Bless, Bardic Inspiration upgrade, Faerie Fire, ...) each have unique knobs — a "generic" endpoint quickly becomes a switchboard. Two separate endpoints with shared _install_buff + _resolve_target_combatant helpers reads cleaner.
**Description (cont 4):** What this unlocks. The (B) Phase B roll-time intercept now has BOTH self-buffs (Rage's damage bonus) AND target-buffs (Hunter's Mark / Hex's weapon-hit riders) to read from. (B) will:
- On weapon /attack: read attacker's buffs for `melee_str_damage_bonus` (Rage) and `weapon_hit_bonus_dice` (Hunter's Mark / Hex). For target-keyed buffs (Hunter's Mark stamps `weapon_hit_bonus_target_combatant_id`), check whether the attack's target combatant matches.
- On save rolls: read attacker's buffs for `advantage_on` lists. For Rage's advantage on STR checks/saves the lookup is straightforward.
- Phase B can ignore concentration-vs-self-buff distinction since the effects fields are identical in shape.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_concentration_buff_for` + `_maybe_concentration_save` + `_resolve_target_combatant` helpers.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_install_buff` concentration swap rule (only one concentration buff per combatant).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `POST /api/campaign/{cid}/cast_hunters_mark` endpoint (Ranger, +1d6 weapon damage rider, concentration).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `POST /api/campaign/{cid}/cast_hex` endpoint (Warlock, +1d6 necrotic rider + ability disadvantage, concentration).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `patch_sheet_fields` calls `_maybe_concentration_save` after applying damage.
- `tests/harness/test_concentration_buffs.py` — 8 new tests covering both endpoints + concentration swap + concentration save on damage. Suite 102 → 110.

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open browser dev console as the GM, run `fetch('/api/campaign/1/cast_hunters_mark', {method:'POST', headers:{'Content-Type':'application/json'}, body:JSON.stringify({character_id: ROWAN_ID, target_character_id: PIP_ID, override:true})})`. A 🎯 chip appears on Rowan in the init tracker reading "Hunter's Mark 100r ×". Apply 10 damage to Rowan via the init-tracker HP input or the sheet — `concentration_save` toast fires; if the d20 missed, the chip disappears. Cast Hex from Magnus on the same target — installs a 🕷️ chip on Magnus. Cast Hunter's Mark on Rowan again with a different target — first chip refreshes target. Cast a second concentration spell on the SAME caster (would require setting up a homebrew or admin scratch character with two concentration spells) — first concentration buff drops.
- **UI wiring deferred.** The sheet's spell row click handler (.sp-cast) doesn't yet special-case Hunter's Mark / Hex to open a target picker before firing /cast_hunters_mark or /cast_hex. Today the demo flow is: open dev console → fetch the endpoint manually. A follow-up commit can intercept .sp-cast for `s._slug === 'hunters-mark' || s._slug === 'hex'` and run a target picker. Filed; doesn't block C.3.
- **Why HP-damage detection is sheet-PATCH-only for v1.** The only is_damage=True caller in the codebase today is `patch_sheet_fields`. Damage applied via init-tracker HP inputs goes through that endpoint already (via the GM's manual HP-cur edit handler). Direct /damage endpoints don't exist; any future ones should call `_maybe_concentration_save` too.
- **CON save proficiency lookup is sheet-driven.** `saving_throws.CON` is the truth (set by class). Demo PCs that have it: Krieger (Barbarian), Garrik (Fighter), Zara (Sorcerer), Magnus (Warlock has WIS/CHA, not CON). Others get CON_mod only.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (C.3) Sheet-side _buffs_active mirror for out-of-combat buff display + sync-on-broadcast. (C.2.1) Sheet-side .sp-cast intercept for Hunter's Mark + Hex with target picker. (B.14+) Phase B roll-time intercepts — Rage damage bonus on attack, Hunter's Mark +1d6 on hit, Hex +1d6 necrotic on hit, Reckless Attack advantage, Colossus Slayer +1d6 on below-max-HP, Sharpshooter -5/+10 uplift.

## [2.19.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase C.1 — buff slot data primitive + Rage as the first user.** Ships the (C) infrastructure for structured timed effects on combatants. Each combatant in the hub battle state gains a `buffs` list; new `_install_buff` / `_remove_buff` / `_get_buffs` helpers mutate it atomically + broadcast `buff_update`. New `/use_rage` endpoint installs a rage buff on the barbarian (10 rounds, +2/+3/+4 damage by level, advantage on STR, resistance to physical) + decrements the Rage counter + marks the bonus chip. New `/end_buff` endpoint lets the owner / GM remove a buff manually. Init tracker renders one purple chip per active buff with a × button to end + a duration counter. GM's turn-advance ticks the ending combatant's buffs by 1 round; buffs at 0 are dropped before the PUT /battle broadcast. Krieger's "Rage" class_features button routes through the new endpoint via `.cf-use`'s `isRage` branch. Effects (damage_bonus / advantage_on / resistance_to) are informational — the (B) Phase B roll-time intercept will read them when applying bonuses at attack/save time. MINOR — new endpoints + new visible UI surface + new buff-list field on combatants.
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new helpers `_install_buff(campaign_id, character_id, buff_dict)` / `_remove_buff(campaign_id, character_id, key)` / `_get_buffs(campaign_id, character_id)` modeled after `_mark_battle_economy`. Each helper iterates `state['combatants']`, finds by `char_id`, mutates the buff list, calls `hub.set_battle` + broadcasts `buff_update` (full list, not a diff, so clients don't track delete-by-key). Install replaces existing buff with same key (refresh semantics — re-casting Rage extends duration cleanly). Plus new `_barbarian_level_from_sheet` + `_rage_damage_bonus(barb_lv)` (Lv 1-8 = +2, Lv 9-15 = +3, Lv 16+ = +4). **(2)** New `/api/campaign/{cid}/use_rage` endpoint — atomic: validates Barbarian Lv 1+ (409), Rage counter ≥ 1 (404/409), Phase 4 over-budget gate on bonus slot (409), decrements counter + commits, installs structured rage buff (key/name/icon/duration/effects), marks bonus chip via `_mark_battle_economy`, broadcasts `feature_used` + `resource_update`. Response carries `{ok, remaining, max, damage_bonus, duration_rounds, buff_installed}`. **(3)** New `/api/campaign/{cid}/end_buff` endpoint — body `{character_id, key}`; auth owner-or-GM; 404 when buff absent. **(4)** `app/templates/tabletop.html` — three additions: (a) `buff_update` WS handler patches `combatant.buffs` from the broadcast list; (b) init-tracker render builds a `buff-chips` row of purple chips with optional duration suffix + a × button; (c) GM's `battle-next-btn` handler decrements `duration_rounds` on every buff whose `source_caster_id` matches the combatant whose turn is ending — buffs hitting 0 are dropped before the PUT /battle pushes the tick'd state. CSS for `.buff-chip` + `.buff-end` (purple-tinted, distinct from the green/amber economy chips; 18 px button per the dense-panel 32 px exception in CLAUDE.md). **(5)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `.cf-use` handler — new `isRage` branch routes Krieger's "Rage" button to `/use_rage`. Success-toast text: "🦬 Rage activated: +N dmg, M rounds (X/Y uses left)". **(6)** `app/demo_seed.py` `seed_encounter` — every combatant now seeds with `buffs: []` so the field is present at first render. **(7)** `tests/harness/test_use_rage.py` — seven new tests covering happy path with buff installation + all three broadcasts, drain-loop to out_of_uses, wrong-class via Pip, missing fields, end_buff happy path, end_buff 404 when absent, end_buff missing fields. Suite grows 95 → 102.
**Description (cont):** Why battle-state buffs (not sheet). User picked "split: battle for timing, sheet for descriptive flags" but Phase C.1 is just the battle-state half. Rage / Hunter's Mark / Hex / Bless are all combat-scoped RAW — Rage ends after 1 minute, Hunter's Mark ends with combat or after 1 hour of concentration, Hex similar. Battle state is the natural home; an encounter ending wipes the active buff slate. The sheet half (descriptive _buffs_active mirror for out-of-combat display) ships in Phase C.3. Storage cost is minimal: hub.battle is an in-memory dict per campaign; adding a 0-element list per combatant is trivial.
**Description (cont 2):** Why GM-driven auto-expire (not server-side timer). The hub's battle state is GM-authoritative — the GM client owns turn advancement + PUT /battle on every tick. Adding a server-side timer would duplicate that authority and force coordination. Instead the GM's existing `nextTurn` handler grows one tick step: for each buff whose `source_caster_id` matches the combatant whose turn just ended (or the combatant itself for self-buffs like Rage where source_caster_id is unset), decrement `duration_rounds`; drop at 0. The decrement happens BEFORE `pushBattle()` pushes the new state — every client sees the tick'd buffs as a single atomic broadcast. v1 deviation: a buff installed mid-round on someone other than the active combatant still ticks down at the active combatant's turn end (only the owner's turn-end matters); this is correct RAW behavior for Rage but not for Hunter's Mark (Hunter's Mark ticks on Rowan's turn-end regardless of who's marked). Phase C.2 will refine when concentration ships.
**Description (cont 3):** Why structured ``effects`` field. The buff carries `{melee_str_damage_bonus, advantage_on, resistance_to}` for Rage. None of these are READ yet — the (B) Phase B roll-time intercept will read them when applying bonuses at attack/save time. Including the data now (rather than waiting for B) means the buff payload is the contract; Phase B's job is just adding the read sites. Alternative considered: install the buff with just `{key, name, duration}` and let Phase B hardcode the effects per key. Rejected because (a) it tangles policy with data, (b) homebrew buffs in a future commit can't define new effect types without a server-side code change. Structured effects + a small lookup vocabulary is more extensible.
**Description (cont 4):** Why GM owns end_buff via direct mutation (NPC path). The /end_buff endpoint requires `character_id` — NPCs without a Character row can't use it. For NPC buffs (a future "GM marks Vex with Hunter's Mark"), the × button in the init tracker falls back to a GM-only local mutation + PUT /battle. The server's _remove_buff helper doesn't care about NPCs since the hub state is just a dict of dicts; only the endpoint's auth wrapper does. Filed: extend /end_buff to accept `combatant_id` instead of `character_id` for NPC support, once NPC buff install lands.
**Description (cont 5):** What this unlocks. Phase C.2 will ship Hunter's Mark + Hex (concentration buffs targeting another combatant) + the concentration save on damage (CON save DC max(10, half damage) when caster takes damage). Phase C.3 will mirror the buff list onto the character sheet so out-of-combat Rage display works. Phase B (roll-time intercept) then reads `combatant.buffs[].effects` to apply the bonuses at attack / save / damage roll time.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `_install_buff` / `_remove_buff` / `_get_buffs` helpers + `_barbarian_level_from_sheet` + `_rage_damage_bonus`.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `POST /api/campaign/{cid}/use_rage` endpoint (atomic counter + buff install + chip mark).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `POST /api/campaign/{cid}/end_buff` endpoint (owner/GM auth).
- `app/templates/tabletop.html` — `buff_update` WS handler; buff badge rendering in init tracker; GM turn-advance auto-expire tick; `.buff-chip` + `.buff-end` CSS.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — `.cf-use` isRage branch routes Krieger's Rage button to `/use_rage`.
- `app/demo_seed.py` — every combatant in `seed_encounter` seeds with `buffs: []`.
- `tests/harness/test_use_rage.py` — 7 tests covering happy path with buff install + all three broadcasts, out_of_uses, wrong-class, missing fields, end_buff happy + 404 + missing fields. Suite 95 → 102.

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open Krieger's mini-sheet, click "Rage" class abilities button → toast "🦬 Rage activated: +2 dmg, 10 rounds (2/3 uses left)" + a purple chip appears under his economy chips reading "🦬 Rage 10r ×" + Bns chip flips. Click the × to end manually; chip disappears. Re-rage, click "Next turn" past Krieger 10 times — chip ticks down then disappears at round 10 with toast "⏱ Rage expired on Krieger". Rage button respects the Phase 4 over-budget modal (try clicking Rage twice in one turn).
- **Why no auto-detection of "no attack / no damage" RAW branch.** Rage RAW says it ends early if your turn ends without attacking or taking damage. The (B) roll-time intercept (Phase B) will set a per-turn flag when Krieger attacks or takes damage; auto-expire can then check that flag at turn-end. For v1 the player ends Rage manually if they didn't attack.
- **Backward compat.** Existing battle states without `buffs` on combatants render fine — the JS coerces missing/null to `[]`. The seed reset re-runs on container restart so live demo state aligns with the new field.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (C.2) Concentration tracking + `/cast_hunters_mark` + `/cast_hex` + concentration save on damage. (C.3) Sheet-side `_buffs_active` mirror. Then (B.14) the (B) Phase B roll-time intercept reads buff effects at attack / save / damage time.
