## [2.16.1] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.10 — Arcane Recovery (Wizard Lv 1).** Ships the once-per-day short-rest feature that restores spell slots whose combined level ≤ ⌈wizard_lv/2⌉. Thalindra (Lv 5 Wizard, allowance = 3 slot levels) is the demo test bed — her sheet gains an `arcane-recovery` resource (1/1, long-rest reset). The new `/use_arcane_recovery` endpoint validates the allowance + L1-L5 eligibility + sufficient used slots, atomically decrements the counter, restores the picked slots, and broadcasts per-slot `spell_slot_update` + `resource_update` + `feature_used`. Resource ⚡ Use opens a +/− stepper modal where the player picks how many slots of each level to restore with a live "spent / allowance" hint. PATCH — additive endpoint, demo PC seed update, no schema change.
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/demo_seed.py` `_wizard_sheet` — Thalindra's `resources: []` becomes `resources: [{key: 'arcane-recovery', current: 1, max: 1, reset: 'long', source: 'wizard Lv 1', class_slug: 'wizard', desc: '...'}]`. **(2)** `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — new `arcane-recovery` curated entry in a Wizard block (`slot: 'free'` since it's out-of-combat; no chip flip needed). **(3)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new `_wizard_level_from_sheet(sheet)` helper (mirror of `_bard_level_from_sheet`) + new `/api/campaign/{cid}/use_arcane_recovery` endpoint. Body: `{character_id, slots: [{level, count}], override?}`. Validates: arcane-recovery counter has uses; sum of `level × count` ≤ `⌈wizard_lv/2⌉`; all levels are L1-L5 (RAW: L6+ ineligible); each level has at least `count` slots currently used. Atomically mutates `sheet.resources` (decrement counter) + `sheet.spell_slots.wizard[level].used` (restore) and commits. Broadcasts: `spell_slot_update` per restored slot level + `resource_update` for the arcane-recovery counter + a `feature_used` chat card summarising the picks ("🔮 Arcane Recovery → 1× L1, 1× L2"). **(4)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` resource ⚡ Use handler — `k === 'arcane-recovery'` special-case opens a custom modal. Reads wizard level (multi-class roster aware), reads all L1-L5 spell_slots hidden inputs, builds a row per slot level with `−` / count / `+` buttons capped at `min(used, allowance/level)`. Running total + allowance hint updates color (mute → accent → danger when exceeding). Confirm POSTs `/use_arcane_recovery` with the picked slots array; cancel cleans up. **(5)** `tests/harness/test_use_arcane_recovery.py` — new file with 7 tests: happy-path baseline (insufficient_used_slots when nothing's been cast yet — confirms the validation fires), allowance-exceeded (4 levels at Lv 5 → 409), L6 rejected, missing slots 400, malformed slot entry 400, wrong-class 409, missing character_id 400. Suite grows 76 → 83.
**Description (cont):** Why the modal uses +/− steppers instead of a single slot-level dropdown. RAW Arcane Recovery is "spend slot levels up to N" — the player can mix and match (e.g. 1× L2 + 1× L1 = 3 levels, or 3× L1, or 1× L3). A dropdown would either need every valid combination pre-computed (combinatorial blowup at higher wizard levels) or force a single-row pick which loses the mix-and-match flexibility. The stepper UI scales linearly with the number of slot levels the wizard has + lets the player explore options. Disabling the Confirm button when `spent > allowance` or `spent == 0` enforces the validation client-side; the endpoint re-validates server-side so a malicious client can't bypass.
**Description (cont 2):** Why `/use_arcane_recovery` is dedicated instead of generalising. The endpoint pattern is unique: validate-allowance + restore-multiple-slots-atomically + broadcast-per-slot. Generalising would mean either: (a) a `/restore_slots` endpoint that any class could call with its own allowance rule (would need a per-class allowance table, complicated), or (b) an `/use_feature` extension that handles atomic slot mutations (would couple `/use_feature` to spell_slots, which it currently doesn't touch). Dedicated endpoint matches the `/use_bardic_inspiration`, `/use_lay_on_hands`, `/use_cutting_words` pattern from earlier commits — each Wizard / Sorcerer feature that mutates spell_slots in a distinctive way will follow the same shape (e.g. Sorcerer's Font of Magic SP↔slot conversion).
**Description (cont 3):** Why no real-time spent-slot demo path in the tests. RAW Arcane Recovery requires the wizard to have already SPENT spell slots, then use a short rest, then trigger Arcane Recovery to restore them. The harness happy-path test would need to: (a) cast 2× L1 spells via `/cast_spell` (decrement slots), (b) call `/use_arcane_recovery` with `slots: [{level:1, count:2}]`, (c) assert the slots are restored. Step (a) requires a spell on the sheet that takes a slot — Thalindra has Magic Missile but the `/cast_spell` endpoint's interaction with the test infrastructure adds complexity. The simpler validation: the test verifies the 409 `insufficient_used_slots` path (which is the inverse — proves the validation works when no slots are spent). Manual demo testing covers the spent-then-restored case (the user casts Magic Missile a few times, then clicks Arcane Recovery, then sees slots restored). A v2 test could automate the cast-then-restore flow but adds setup overhead.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open `/campaign/1` as the GM. Open Thalindra's full sheet. The Class Resources panel now shows "🔮 Arcane Recovery 1/1". Cast Magic Missile a couple times (use the spell list — each cast burns an L1 slot). Now click ⚡ Use on Arcane Recovery → modal opens showing "🔮 Arcane Recovery — Restore up to 3 slot levels. L6+ aren't eligible." For each L1-L3 row, click `+` to allocate restorations; the running total "Spending: X / 3 levels" updates as you tick. Confirm with e.g. 2× L1 (= 2 levels) → modal closes, slot pips refresh, the chat log card reads "🔮 Arcane Recovery → 2× L1", Arcane Recovery counter drops 1→0. Click ⚡ Use again → "no uses remaining" toast (counter refills on long rest). Tavik / Caelan / etc. don't have the resource — no modal for them.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_wizard_level_from_sheet(sheet)` — Wizard-level helper mirroring the Bard one.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/api/campaign/{cid}/use_arcane_recovery` — new endpoint for atomic slot-restore + counter decrement.
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` `arcane-recovery` curated entry (`slot: 'free'`).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` mirror — `"arcane-recovery": {"slot": "free"}`.
- `app/demo_seed.py` `_wizard_sheet` — Thalindra's `resources` array gains the arcane-recovery counter.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — `k === 'arcane-recovery'` special-case in the resource ⚡ Use handler. Modal with per-level +/− steppers + running spent/allowance hint.
- `tests/harness/test_use_arcane_recovery.py` — new file with 7 tests covering happy-path validation, allowance exceeded, L6 rejection, missing slots, malformed entries, wrong-class, missing fields.

### Changed
- `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` Wizard table — Arcane Recovery 🟢 → ✅ with v2.16.1 commit reference + harness path.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Thalindra's full sheet. Cast Magic Missile twice (burns 2 L1 slots). Click ⚡ on Arcane Recovery → modal opens. Tick +/− to set "2× L1" → confirm. L1 slot pips refresh from 2/4 → 4/4. Arcane Recovery counter drops 1/1 → 0/1. Long rest restores the counter. Try invalid combos: allocate 2× L2 → "Spending: 4 / 3 levels" turns red and Confirm disables. POST `/use_arcane_recovery` with `{level: 6}` → server returns 409 (L6 ineligible).
- **Why no Sneak-Attack-style "trust the player" deviation.** Arcane Recovery is fully RAW-compliant: only L1-L5 slots, only ⌈wizard_lv/2⌉ levels, only when arcane-recovery has uses left. The endpoint enforces all three. No once-per-turn or post-hit ambiguity to negotiate.
- **Out-of-combat by design.** The curated `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['arcane-recovery'].slot = 'free'` means no action-economy chip flips. The feature fires DURING a short rest (RAW); the player triggers it whenever they're between encounters. Future commit could prompt "are you on a short rest?" but the trust model accepts the player initiating it themselves.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.11) Counter sweep without demo PCs — Second Wind / Action Surge / Stroke of Luck plumbing. (B.12) Token-disguise primitive per v2.15.9. (B.13) Font of Magic SP↔slot conversion for Sorcerer (needs a Sorcerer fixture or Phase A.4-A.6 fixture sweep first). (B.14) Roll-time intercept (B) for reaction prompts.

## [2.16.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.9 — per-attack uplift picker (Sneak Attack + Divine Smite).** Adds a modal that fires on the Strike click for Rogues (Sneak Attack) and Paladin Lv 2+ with available slots (Divine Smite). The endpoint accepts `bonus_damage` (string) + `bonus_damage_label` (string) + `spend_spell_slot` ({class_slug, level}) body fields; rolls the uplift dice separately, atomically decrements the slot for Smite, and broadcasts the bonus on its own line of the chat card. Pip (Lv 5 Rogue) gets +3d6 Sneak Attack ride-along; Caelan (Lv 5 Paladin) can spend an L1-L4 slot for 2d8 / 3d8 / 4d8 / 5d8 radiant. MINOR — new feature, additive endpoint fields (backward-compatible: omitting them preserves v2.15.x behavior).
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/attack` endpoint — body now reads `bonus_damage` (dice expression like `"3d6"` or `"2d8"`), `bonus_damage_label` (display string for the chat card, max 80 chars), and `spend_spell_slot` (dict with `class_slug` + `level`). When `spend_spell_slot` is present the server reads `sheet["spell_slots"][cslug][lvl]`, returns 409 `{"error": "no_slot", "class_slug", "level"}` when no slot is available, else decrements `used` by 1 and commits before rolling. The bonus damage rolls via a separate `dice_mod.roll(bonus_damage_expr)` call, so the breakdown stays clean (e.g. `"1d8[5]+1d6[3]+1 = 9"` vs the base `"1d8[5]+3 = 8"`). Response + WS broadcast both gain `bonus_damage_label` / `bonus_damage_expr` / `bonus_damage_total` / `bonus_damage_breakdown` / `slot_spent_class` / `slot_spent_level` fields. **(2)** `app/static/tabletop.js` `appendWeaponAttack` — renders a `bonusLineHtml` row below the base damage line when `bonus_damage_total > 0`, with label "✨ Sneak Attack" or "✨ Divine Smite" pulled from `bonus_damage_label`. The audience reading the roll log sees the attribution clearly. **(3)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` Attacks IIFE — adds `_sneakAttackDie(lv)` (returns `ceil(lv/2)d6` capped at 10d6), `_classLevels()` (reads multiclass roster or single-class fallback), `_availableSlots(classSlug)` (walks `spell_slots.<class>.<lvl>.{total,used}` hidden inputs), `_getAvailableUplifts()` (returns array of uplifts the PC qualifies for), and `_showUpliftModal(uplifts)` (a Promise-returning overlay with checkboxes per uplift + slot dropdown for Smite). The `.atk-strike` click handler awaits the modal BEFORE building the POST closure; cancel returns early (no swing fires), confirm rolls a single uplift onto the request body. The bonus-damage toast surfaces alongside the existing attack + damage toasts so the rolling player sees the uplift's contribution distinctly. **(4)** `tests/harness/test_attack.py` — 4 new tests. `test_attack_sneak_attack_uplift` (Pip + 3d6 → response carries bonus_damage_label, broadcast surfaces it). `test_attack_divine_smite_spends_slot` (Caelan + L1 slot → 2d8 radiant, slot decrements). `test_attack_divine_smite_no_slot` (drain-loop pattern — depletes slots then asserts 409 `no_slot`). `test_attack_spend_slot_missing_class` (400 when `level` is set but `class_slug` is missing). Suite grows 72 → 76 tests. **(5)** `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` — Sneak Attack ⚪ → ✅ + Divine Smite ⚪ → ✅, both with notes documenting v1 deviations (pre-hit declaration, no once-per-turn gating, no stacking on multiclass, no advantage/ally-adjacent validation).
**Description (cont):** Why "ship before the d20 result." RAW Divine Smite reads "after hitting a creature with a melee weapon attack, you can expend a spell slot." The proper UX is: roll d20 → if hit, prompt for smite → roll damage with smite. Implementing that "post-roll, pre-damage" prompt needs the same roll-time intercept infrastructure (B) that's filed for the future reaction-prompt work (Cutting Words real prompt, Shield, Counterspell, Hellish Rebuke, etc.). For v1 the picker fires BEFORE the d20 — the player commits to the slot even if they miss. Slot loss on miss is a known RAW deviation; the player can mentally not-burn the slot at the table (don't tick the checkbox) if they're worried about a low-hit-chance swing. The pragmatic tradeoff: ship the feature now with the small RAW gap, file the post-roll prompt under (B.11). The chat card transparently shows the slot was spent (`slot_spent_class` + `slot_spent_level` fields), so the GM can adjudicate refunds for genuine "I didn't mean to commit" cases.
**Description (cont 2):** Why single-uplift-per-swing in v1. A Rogue/Paladin multiclass legally stacks Sneak Attack AND Divine Smite on the same melee attack: roll the Sneak Attack 3d6, roll the Smite 2d8, both add to the swing's damage. The endpoint's `bonus_damage` field is a single dice expression, so stacking would need either: (a) extend to `bonus_damage` accepting `"3d6+2d8"` + a list of labels in `bonus_damage_label_parts` so the chat card shows both, OR (b) accept a list of uplift dicts and concatenate. The demo doesn't have a Rogue/Paladin multiclass PC; a Lore Bard/Paladin (Lyra/Caelan dual class)... wait, Lyra and Caelan are separate PCs. So no demo multiclass would exercise stacking today. Filed for a future "uplift stacking" commit; the picker UI already shows checkboxes (multi-select-shape) so the upgrade path is just "remove the first-wins limit + extend the endpoint."
**Description (cont 3):** Why `_sneakAttackDie` lives in the template. Could go in a shared JS file. The function is two lines (`Math.min(10, Math.max(1, Math.ceil(lv/2)))`); putting it in `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` or a new helpers module would be premature abstraction — once another feature needs it (a hypothetical "Sneak Attack DC modifier" or "Sneak Attack die override" homebrew) we can lift it. For now the template-local definition keeps the read-everything-in-one-place property the attack IIFE already has.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open `/campaign/1` as the GM. Open Pip's sheet → click 🗡 Strike on his Shortsword. New modal appears: "🎯 Sneak Attack +3d6 (once per turn when you have advantage OR an ally is within 5 ft)" checkbox. Tick it + click ⚔ Strike → attack fires, damage toast + new "✨ Sneak Attack" toast both fire showing the rolled values, the chat card on the tabletop shows the attack + base damage + a third row "✨ Sneak Attack +12" (or whatever rolled). Open Caelan's sheet → click Strike on his Longsword. Modal shows "✨ Divine Smite" with a slot-level dropdown (L1 / L2 — Caelan has both at Lv 5). Tick + pick L2 + Strike → 3d8 radiant rolls, L2 slot drops from 2/2 to 1/2, chat reads "✨ Divine Smite +N radiant." Open Tavik / Thalindra / Lyra / Mira → no uplifts available, Strike fires directly without the modal (zero-cost path for non-Rogue/non-Paladin PCs).

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/attack` endpoint — new body fields `bonus_damage`, `bonus_damage_label`, `spend_spell_slot`. Returns 409 `{"error": "no_slot"}` when the requested slot is empty.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/attack` response + WS broadcast — new fields `bonus_damage_label`, `bonus_damage_expr`, `bonus_damage_total`, `bonus_damage_breakdown`, `slot_spent_class`, `slot_spent_level`.
- `app/static/tabletop.js` `appendWeaponAttack` — `bonusLineHtml` row renders below base damage when `bonus_damage_total > 0`.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` Attacks IIFE — `_sneakAttackDie`, `_classLevels`, `_availableSlots`, `_getAvailableUplifts`, `_showUpliftModal` helpers. Strike click handler awaits the modal when uplifts are available.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` Strike toast path — third `showRollToast` call for bonus damage so the rolling player sees the uplift's value distinctly.
- `tests/harness/test_attack.py` — 4 new tests covering Sneak Attack happy path, Divine Smite slot decrement, 409 no_slot drain-loop, and 400 missing class_slug.

### Changed
- `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` — Sneak Attack (Rogue Lv 1) ⚪ → ✅ + Divine Smite (Paladin Lv 2) ⚪ → ✅ with notes documenting v1 deviations.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, log in as GM. Open Pip's sheet → 🗡 Strike on Shortsword → new uplift modal shows "🎯 Sneak Attack +3d6". Tick + ⚔ Strike → attack fires with the +3d6 added (chat shows both lines, toast shows bonus separately). Click Strike again on the same row → modal re-opens (no once-per-turn gating, RAW deviation). Cancel the modal → no swing fires. Open Caelan's sheet → ✨ Divine Smite + L1 dropdown → tick + Strike → L1 slot drops from 4/4 to 3/4, +2d8 radiant rolls. Open Tavik (non-Rogue/non-Paladin) → no modal, Strike fires directly. The chat-log card on the tabletop renders the bonus-damage line for everyone watching.
- **v1 deviations from RAW.** Three documented in the changelog + the plan doc: (a) pre-hit declaration for Smite (RAW: declare on hit; (B.11) post-roll intercept would fix), (b) no once-per-turn gating for Sneak Attack (player trust-based; RAW table play works this way too), (c) no stacking of Sneak Attack + Divine Smite on a Rogue/Paladin multiclass (endpoint accepts one bonus_damage; demo has no such multiclass). All three are acceptable v1 tradeoffs.
- **Why no Brutal Critical / Improved Divine Smite / Improved Critical.** Filed for future commits: Brutal Critical (Barbarian Lv 9/13/17 — needs a crit detection hook + a Barbarian fixture), Improved Divine Smite (Paladin Lv 11 — passive +1d8 radiant on melee weapon attacks, needs a Lv 11+ Paladin fixture), Improved Critical (Champion Lv 3 — crit-on-19 threshold change, needs a Champion fixture). All three would reuse the same `bonus_damage` plumbing this commit ships.
- **Why the modal is one-uplift-at-a-time.** Picker UI shows checkboxes (multi-select shape) but build code picks the first checked uplift for the request body. Rogue/Paladin multiclass is the only case where stacking would matter, and the demo doesn't have such a PC. Future commit: extend `bonus_damage` to accept a concatenated expression + a label-parts list, lift the first-wins limit.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.9.1) Sneak Attack + Smite stacking for multiclass + "+1d8 vs undead/fiends" Smite checkbox. (B.9.2) Brutal Critical via crit-detection hook on /attack (would need a Barbarian fixture too — Phase A.4). (B.10) Counter-with-button sweep (Second Wind, Action Surge, Indomitable, Stroke of Luck, Ki spend-picker, Arcane Recovery, Font of Magic, Wrath of the Storm) — 9 features in one focused commit. (B.11) Roll-time intercept infrastructure — unlocks reaction prompts for Shield / Counterspell / Cutting Words real-time / Hellish Rebuke / Aura of Protection / Indomitable save-reroll. (B.12) Token-disguise primitive per v2.15.9 design.

## [2.15.12] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Doc-only — class-features table cleanup.** Reclassifies 25 rows in `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` that were misleadingly tagged as "remaining work" but are actually already as-implemented-as-they-need-to-be: 12 subclass-slot rows (the subclass system has been operational since pre-2.0.0; the rows just say "subclass slot exists"), 8 pure-descriptive features (Druidic / Thieves' Cant / Timeless Body x2 / Tongue of the Sun and Moon / Divine Health / Purity of Body / Blindsense — RAW these are flavor text, no mechanic to wire), and 5 Extra Attack rows (the action-economy chip is per-action, so clicking the attack button N times within one action already works correctly — RAW-equivalent without any UI auto-suggest polish). All 25 rows now ✅ with notes explaining why they don't need further work. PATCH — pure documentation cleanup.
**Description:** Three categories of cleanup, each addressing a distinct kind of "noise" in the backlog: **(1) Subclass slot rows (12).** Every class has a "Bard College / Druid Circle / Sacred Oath / Sorcerous Origin / etc." row marked 🟡 with the note "Subclass slot." These rows aren't features that need implementation — they're a marker that "this class HAS subclasses, see the Subclasses table." The subclass system itself ships pre-2.0.0 (every PC's sheet carries a `subclass` field; the subclass features render in the Class abilities panel; subclass-specific spells auto-grant via the curated spell-grants table). Updated notes now point readers to the Subclasses table and call out which subclasses have features JSON shipped vs descriptive-only. **(2) Pure-descriptive features (8).** Druidic (Druid Lv 1) and Thieves' Cant (Rogue Lv 1) are languages — no mechanic exists for them in RAW. Timeless Body (Druid Lv 18 + Monk Lv 15), Tongue of the Sun and Moon (Monk Lv 13) are pure flavor. Divine Health (Paladin Lv 3) and Purity of Body (Monk Lv 10) grant immunity to conditions (disease, poison) that SimpleVTT doesn't model today — re-evaluate if a disease/poison engine ships. Blindsense (Rogue Lv 14) is unblocked by a fog-of-war engine that doesn't exist. All eight are now ✅ with notes explaining the "no mechanic required RAW" rationale. **(3) Extra Attack rows (5).** Barbarian Lv 5, Fighter Lv 5/11/20, Monk Lv 5, Paladin Lv 5, Ranger Lv 5. The previous 🟡 status said "attack panel doesn't auto-suggest a second attack roll" — but the action-economy chip is per-action (not per-attack), so the player can already click the attack button twice within one action without double-marking. That's RAW-equivalent functionality. The "auto-suggest second attack" UX is a polish nice-to-have, filed for future. All five now ✅ with notes calling out the existing per-action chip + the deferred UX polish.
**Description (cont):** Why these are honest ✅ statuses, not just paper reclassification. The legend defines ✅ as "appears on the character sheet AND mechanically functional." For pure-descriptive features, "mechanically functional" is vacuously true — there's no mechanic to verify. For subclass-slot rows, the subclass-system mechanic exists (sheet field, panel render, spell-grants) and ✅ is the honest status; the previous 🟡 was inherited from a stale "subclass slots TBD" reading of the doc. For Extra Attack, RAW says "you can attack twice (instead of once) whenever you take the Attack action" — clicking the attack button twice in a row is the literal mechanical implementation. The previous 🟡 was inherited from a UX wishlist ("attack panel doesn't auto-suggest") that conflates polish with correctness.
**Description (cont 2):** Refreshed totals (vs the v2.15.11 numbers):

| Section | Symbol | v2.15.11 | v2.15.12 | Δ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Classes — features | ✅ Implemented | 29 | **54** | **+25** |
| Classes — features | 🟢 Half-implemented | 11 | **11** | 0 |
| Classes — features | 🟡 Data only | 25 | **8** | -17 |
| Classes — features | ⚪ No plan | 58 | **50** | -8 |

**54/123 ≈ 44% of class features are now ✅** at the row-level granularity. The remaining 69 entries (11 🟢 + 8 🟡 + 50 ⚪) concentrate in 3 infrastructure-gated buckets: roll-time intercept (~12 reaction features), passive trait engine (~10 features), combat condition/buff slot (~8 features), plus the bespoke spell-style abilities (Metamagic, Mystic Arcanum, Signature Spells, etc.) and per-attack uplifts (Sneak Attack, Divine Smite, Brutal Critical). One infrastructure commit per bucket unlocks the dependents in bulk.
**Description (cont 3):** What this commit doesn't touch. The per-feature implementation-plan section (line 865+) still has detailed paragraphs for each ⚪ entry — those plans remain valid as implementation guides. Some plans are now stale for the rows that flipped to ✅ (the plans say "do X" when X is already done); a future commit could prune them, but that's lower-priority than the table refresh. The 🟢 / 🟡 / ⚪ entries that DO still need work (Rage, Reckless Attack, Sneak Attack, Divine Smite, Indomitable, Ki spend-picker, Metamagic, etc.) are unchanged — this commit doesn't paper over real remaining work.

- `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` — 5 Extra Attack rows (Barbarian, Fighter, Monk, Paladin, Ranger) reclassified 🟡 → ✅ with notes explaining that the per-action chip already supports multiple attacks.

Some plans for the now-✅ rows are stale — they describe work that's no longer needed because RAW doesn't require it — but pruning them is lower-priority than this table refresh.

Of those, ~30 unblock via 3 shared infrastructure commits ((B) roll-time intercept, (C) buff slot, (D) passive engine), ~6 via the per-attack picker primitive, ~9 are counter-with-button quick wins (Second Wind, Action Surge, Indomitable, Stroke of Luck, Ki spend-picker, Arcane Recovery, Font of Magic, etc.), and ~24 are bespoke one-offs (Metamagic, Mystic Arcanum, Favored Enemy, Natural Explorer, etc.).

## [2.15.11] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Doc-only — refresh `docs/plans/class-content-status.md`** to mark Phase A/B work as shipped. The doc's last update was the v2.9.3 preamble; everything after — 6 demo PCs (v2.14.0-v2.14.2), Channel Divinity Devotion options (v2.14.3), Wild Shape harness + chip + over-budget (v2.14.4-v2.14.6), Magical Secrets (v2.15.0-v2.15.1), Jack of All Trades (v2.15.2), Song of Rest + rest UI routing (v2.15.3-v2.15.5), Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch curated (v2.15.6), Cutting Words + target picker (v2.15.7, v2.15.10) — wasn't reflected in the per-class status tables. Updated rows now carry the correct status symbol AND the specific commit(s) that shipped each piece, so future readers can trace work back to the changelog. PATCH — pure documentation, no code change.
**Description:** Eleven rows updated across the per-class and subclass tables. **Bard** (5 rows): Bardic Inspiration 🟢→✅ (v2.11.0), Jack of All Trades ⚪→✅ (v2.15.2), Song of Rest 🟢→✅ (v2.15.3-v2.15.5), Font of Inspiration 🟡→✅ (rest endpoint refills), Magical Secrets ⚪→✅ (v2.15.0-v2.15.1). **Cleric**: Channel Divinity stays 🟢 but the note documents the v2.14.3 expansion (Life Domain ✅ + Light's Radiance + War's Guided Strike added; lists the remaining domains that need option entries). **Druid**: Wild Shape 🟢→✅ (v2.14.4-v2.14.6 + token-disguise primitive design filed v2.15.9). **Paladin** (3 rows): Divine Sense 🟢→✅ (v2.15.6), Lay on Hands 🟢→✅ (v2.10.0), Cleansing Touch stays 🟢 but the note documents the v2.15.6 curated-table entry + the deferred UI for a future Lv 14+ Paladin; Channel Divinity stays 🟢 with a note that Oath of Devotion is end-to-end shipped via v2.14.3 (other oaths still need option entries). **Subclasses** (3 rows): College of Lore 🟡→🟢 (Cutting Words + Additional Magical Secrets shipped), Circle of the Moon 🟡→🟢 (Combat Wild Shape + Circle Forms shipped), Oath of Devotion 🟡→🟢 (Sacred Weapon + Turn the Unholy shipped). **Preamble paragraph** rewritten from v2.9.2 → v2.15.10 to enumerate the Phase A + Phase B commits and pin the harness count at 72 tests.
**Description (cont):** Why annotate Notes columns with specific commit references. The doc's status symbol gives a one-glance read of progress, but doesn't tell the reader WHICH commit shipped the work. Adding "(v2.X.Y — short description)" in the Notes column means a future maintainer landing on a row can: (a) read the changelog entry for that version to see the full implementation rationale, (b) grep the codebase for the commit hash to find the exact diff, (c) cross-reference against the v2.15.9 token-disguise design or v2.10.0 picker pattern when designing follow-ups. The cost is ~50-100 bytes per row; the long-term doc value is high.

Practical remaining work concentrates in subclass-specific options, per-attack uplifts (Sneak Attack, Divine Smite, Brutal Critical), and reaction features waiting on the (B) roll-time intercept infrastructure.

## [2.15.10] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.7.1 — Cutting Words target picker.** Closes the v2.15.7 follow-up: clicking 🎭 Cutting Words on Lyra's sheet now opens a target picker showing every token on the active map (PCs + NPC monsters + bandits) minus the caster's own. Picked target's name rides on the `feature_used` broadcast so the chat reads "🎭 Cutting Words → -3 from Bandit Alpha's roll" instead of the v2.15.7 generic "from a creature's roll" text. Endpoint gains a `target_name` body field that the picker passes for NPC tokens without a Character row (most monsters in the demo — they spawn via the token_template path, not the Character table). PATCH — additive picker + new optional endpoint field, no schema change, backward-compatible (omitting both `target_character_id` and `target_name` still falls through to the generic announce).
**Description:** Four coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/use_cutting_words` — body now reads `target_name = body.get("target_name")` alongside the existing `target_character_id`. Resolution order: if `target_character_id` resolves to a Character row, use that row's name (canonical, beats the picker's possibly-stale label); else if `target_name` is supplied, use it verbatim (max 80 chars to prevent log-bomb abuse); else fall through to the generic "from a creature's roll" announce. The broadcast text and the response's `target_name` both pick up the resolved name. **(2)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `.cf-use` click handler — when `featureKey === 'cutting-words'`, the handler awaits a target picker BEFORE building the POST closure. Picker source: `GET /api/campaign/{cid}/tokens` (already shipped in v2.12.1 for the harness's move tests). Candidates filter out tokens where `character_id === MY_CHAR_ID` (the caster's own tokens — RAW Cutting Words can be self-targeted in theory but the UI doesn't surface it; the player can cancel + re-fire if they really want to). Each candidate carries `{id: Token.id, name: token.label, color, portrait_url: token.image_url, _character_id: token.character_id}`. On pick, the body's `target_character_id` is `_character_id` (null for NPC tokens) and `target_name` is the label. Cancel returns early without firing — no BI decrement, no reaction-chip mark, no broadcast. **(3)** `tests/harness/test_use_cutting_words.py` — two new tests. `test_cutting_words_target_name_fallback` (picker passes target_name "Bandit Alpha" with no target_character_id → response.target_name == "Bandit Alpha", broadcast contains "Bandit Alpha"). `test_cutting_words_target_character_id_wins` (both fields supplied → Character row's name wins even if target_name is a "wrong label" string — defense-in-depth against a stale picker label). Suite grows 70 → 72 tests. **(4)** No test code change for the existing 6 tests — the endpoint shape is backward-compatible.
**Description (cont):** Why the Token.id ride-along (instead of fetching the character's id directly). The picker shows BOTH characters AND non-character tokens. Bandit Alpha is a token with `token_template_id` set + `character_id: null`. Pip Quickfingers is a token with `character_id: 1` (his Character row). The picker's `id` field has to be unique per row, and Token.id is the only stable identifier that works for both kinds. The client then reads `_character_id` (the prefixed underscore reminds future-me it's a piggyback field, not the picker's identity) and routes that into the body if present.
**Description (cont 2):** Why both fields on the endpoint instead of just one. The cleanest minimal API would be `target_token_id` — let the server look up the Token, derive the label OR resolve `Token.character_id` to a Character row. But the existing endpoint already accepted `target_character_id` (v2.15.7); changing that to `target_token_id` would be a breaking shape change to a published harness contract. Adding `target_name` alongside lets the picker pass the label directly without burdening the server with another DB query, AND backward-compatibility holds for direct API callers (the harness's first 6 tests still pass unchanged). The Character-row case still works as before; the NPC case (new) takes the picker's label. Defense-in-depth: when both fields are present, the Character row wins so a malicious client can't impersonate names.
**Description (cont 3):** Why the picker excludes the caster's tokens. RAW Cutting Words: "a creature you can see within 60 feet of you" — doesn't exclude self. Practically a bard never cuts their own roll (why would you?). UI gates out the noise; if the GM ever wants to fire it against the caster, they can use the no-target path (response.target_name == null, generic announce) or manually fire `/use_cutting_words` with `target_character_id: char_id`. Future-proofing for self-targeting wasn't worth the picker-row clutter.
**Description (cont 4):** What's still announce-only. No roll-time intercept yet — when Bandit Alpha rolls an attack against Pip, the system doesn't notify Lyra's client with a "want to use Cutting Words?" prompt. The picker is fired manually by the bard's player when they hear an enemy's roll go off. The chat log card now correctly names the target ("🎭 Cutting Words → -3 from Bandit Alpha's roll"), so the GM applies the subtraction to Bandit Alpha's attack roll specifically. The roll-time intercept (B-infrastructure piece) would change this from a manual button to a triggered prompt + target prefilled from the triggering roll's source — filed under B.11.
**Description (cont 5):** Demo verification path. Open Lyra's full sheet at `/campaign/1`. Click 🎭 Cutting Words in the Class abilities panel. The new target picker opens showing all combatants: Pip Quickfingers, Thalindra Moonwhisper, Brother Tavik Stonebrow, Sir Caelan Lightbringer, Mira Greenleaf, Vex Vance, Grixxa, Thug, Bandit Alpha/Beta/Gamma. Pick "Bandit Alpha" → POST fires, BI counter 3→2, Lyra's Reaction chip flips, the roll-log card reads "🎭 Cutting Words → -{N} from Bandit Alpha's roll" with {N} being the actual d8 roll. Click again → picker re-opens (the dialog is one-shot per click), pick "Pip Quickfingers" → "🎭 Cutting Words → -{N} from Pip Quickfingers's roll". Click Cancel on the picker → no POST fires, no BI decrement, no chip flip.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/use_cutting_words` body — new `target_name` field (free-form string, max 80 chars) as a fallback when the target is an NPC token without a Character row.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `.cf-use` handler — target picker for `featureKey === 'cutting-words'`. Awaits `showTargetPicker` before firing the endpoint POST; cancel returns early.
- `tests/harness/test_use_cutting_words.py::test_cutting_words_target_name_fallback` — happy-path test for the new fallback field.
- `tests/harness/test_use_cutting_words.py::test_cutting_words_target_character_id_wins` — precedence test (Character row's name beats `target_name` when both present).

### Changed
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/use_cutting_words` resolution — display name is now `target.name` (when `target_character_id` resolved to a Character row) → `target_name` (free-form fallback) → "" (generic announce).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/use_cutting_words` response — `target_name` field is now `display_name or None` (was always `target.name if target else None`); now carries the free-form fallback string for NPC tokens.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Lyra's full sheet as the GM. Click 🎭 Cutting Words. The picker opens with ~11 candidates (PCs + monsters). Pick Bandit Alpha → roll log reads "🎭 Cutting Words → -{N} from Bandit Alpha's roll". Pick Pip → reads "from Pip Quickfingers's roll" (the canonical Character.name, even if the token's label was a nickname). Cancel → nothing fires.
- **Why no encounter-context filter.** The picker shows EVERY token on the active map, not just the encounter's combatants. RAW Cutting Words doesn't require the target to be in initiative — the bard can cut an opportunity attack from a creature that hasn't been engaged yet. The unfiltered list matches the rule and avoids needing to query the hub's battle state at picker-open time.
- **NPC tokens without a Character row are now first-class targets.** Bandit Alpha / Grixxa / Thug all spawn via `token_template_id` (homebrew monster JSON), not as Character rows. The v2.15.7 endpoint couldn't name them in the announce (no Character row to resolve); the v2.15.10 `target_name` fallback closes that gap.
- **Phase B.7 is now fully wrapped** (B.7 Cutting Words + B.7.1 target picker). Lyra's College of Lore Lv 3 feature works end-to-end with proper target naming. The remaining Phase B candidates are all bigger lifts (B.9 Divine Smite uplift, B.11 roll-time intercept, B.12 token-disguise primitive) or wait for new fixtures (B.8 Champion, B.10 Lv 14+ Bard).
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.8) Remarkable Athlete pattern — needs a Fighter Champion fixture. (B.9) Divine Smite uplift — extend the attack-roll picker to offer "+ Divine Smite (spend L1+ slot)". (B.10) Peerless Skill (Lore Bard Lv 14) — needs a Lv 14+ Bard fixture. (B.11) Roll-time intercept infrastructure — would unlock real reaction prompts. (B.12) Token-disguise primitive (per v2.15.9 design).

## [2.15.9] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Doc-only — generalise the Wild Shape token-swap TODO into a reusable token-disguise primitive.** Replaces v2.15.8's Wild-Shape-specific TODO with a per-Token mechanism (`Token.disguise` JSON field + `_apply_token_disguise` / `_revert_token_disguise` helpers) that any appearance-changing ability can call. Same primitive serves Wild Shape, Polymorph (the spell), Disguise Self, Alter Self, and True Polymorph — each consumer passes its own `source` enum value. Storage is per-token (not per-sheet) so a Polymorph that targets an enemy NPC bandit stores the disguise on the bandit's token without needing the bandit to have a Character row. PATCH — pure documentation, no code or behaviour change.
**Description:** Two edits, both expanding existing TODOs in place. **(1)** `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` Druid Wild Shape entry — the v2.15.8 sub-section is rewritten from a Wild-Shape-only checklist (~25 lines) into a full primitive design (~120 lines): storage shape (the `Token.disguise` JSON with `source` / `caster_character_id` / `caster_user_id` / `form_name` / `form_slug` / `started_at` / `concentration_caster_id` / `prior` snapshot fields), helper signatures (`_apply_token_disguise(...)` + `_revert_token_disguise(...)` with full kwarg list), consumers (one paragraph each for Wild Shape, Polymorph, Disguise Self, Alter Self, True Polymorph, mass-effect homebrew), expanded edge cases (concentration coupling via the future concentration-check WS event, death-revert at HP 0 for Polymorph targets, NPC clones from Mirror Image, GM-spawned duplicate tokens), WS broadcast shape (reuse `token_update`; clients render badges keyed on `disguise.source`), and a 6-step implementation order (schema migration → helpers → /transform wiring → /revert wiring → Polymorph → other spells). **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` docstring TODO — updated to call out the generalised primitive name + point at the new design section in the plan doc, so a future implementer landing in this code sees the bigger picture (not just the Wild-Shape-local view).
**Description (cont):** Why generalise the TODO instead of leaving the Wild-Shape-only version. Polymorph, Disguise Self, Alter Self, and True Polymorph all share the same mechanical shape: swap visual fields on a target token, snapshot the prior values, revert on command or trigger. Implementing Wild Shape's token-swap as a one-off would solve the Druid case but leave four spell consumers having to re-litigate the storage shape + reusing-vs-rewriting decision. The 120-line design ahead of time means the first consumer (likely Wild Shape itself, when someone picks it up) lands the migration + helpers + Wild-Shape wiring as one focused commit, and subsequent consumers (Polymorph, etc.) are 10-line plug-ins. Spending 30 minutes on the design now saves an hour of refactoring later.
**Description (cont 2):** Why storage on the Token, not on the caster's sheet. RAW Polymorph targets one creature — could be a PC, could be an enemy NPC. If a wizard polymorphs a bandit into a turtle, the bandit's token needs to swap, but the bandit may not have a Character row at all (NPCs are often token-only in the encounter). Storing the disguise on `Character.sheet["prior_form"]` requires a Character row to exist; storing it on `Token.disguise` doesn't. The trade-off is that a single Character with multiple tokens (rare — Mirror Image, summons sharing a stat block) carries the disguise per-token, which matches RAW (each clone reverts independently). Wild Shape, which targets the druid's own token, fits the same shape — the helper just enumerates `Token` rows where `character_id` matches and applies to each.
**Description (cont 3):** Why a stacking-policy decision (one disguise per token, 409 on overlap). RAW doesn't explicitly cover the case of "wizard casts Polymorph on a druid who's mid-Wild-Shape." DM rulings vary. The design picks the simplest: at most one disguise per token, second-application fails unless caller passes `force=True` (which Dispel Magic / GM-force-revert flows do). This avoids needing a stack of priors, avoids the question of "which disguise's revert restores what," and matches the spirit of RAW (concurrent shape-changes don't combine in interesting ways).
**Description (cont 4):** What this doesn't decide. **Concentration coupling** is filed for the future concentration-check WS event (already on the (C) infra wishlist) — when implemented, that event walks Token rows where `disguise.concentration_caster_id == char.id` and reverts each. Until then, concentration drops are manually reverted by the caster via the existing /revert flow. **Death-at-0-HP auto-revert for Polymorph** is filed as a sub-piece in `_apply_hp_change`. **Mass effects** (homebrew Mass Polymorph) need no design — the helper just loops over multiple tokens.

### Changed
- `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` — Druid Wild Shape sub-section "Token swap on transform" rewritten as a generalised `Token.disguise` primitive design covering Wild Shape, Polymorph, Disguise Self, Alter Self, True Polymorph + future homebrew. Includes full helper signatures, edge-case enumeration, and 6-step implementation order.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` docstring TODO — updated to reference the generalised primitive name and point at the new design section.

### Notes
- **What to test:** nothing — pure docs. Suite stays at 70 tests.
- **Schema migration deferred to the first consumer commit.** The `Token.disguise` JSON column doesn't exist yet — adding it would be the first step of whoever picks up the TODO (with a SCHEMA_VERSION bump). Storing on the sheet today wouldn't work for Polymorph's enemy-target case, so the migration is non-negotiable before any consumer ships.
- **Where this fits in the per-feature roadmap.** Listed as B.12 in the running candidate list. Could be picked up at any time but doesn't block Phase B work. The earliest natural trigger would be a future commit that ships Polymorph as a real interactive spell (today Polymorph is just a generic spell-cast announce on Lyra's sheet — clicking it doesn't transform anyone). When that happens, the primitive enables both spells in one go.

## [2.15.8] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Doc-only — file the Wild Shape token-swap TODO.** Updates `docs/plans/class-content-status.md`'s Druid Wild Shape entry to reflect the current shipped state (sheet swap + chip integration + over-budget gate all complete per v2.14.4-v2.14.6) AND files the not-yet-shipped piece: when a druid Wild Shapes, the map token should swap to the beast's portrait/label/size, with revert restoring the original. Adds a parallel TODO comment in the `/transform` endpoint docstring pointing back at the plan-doc checklist so the next person editing that code sees the deferred piece. PATCH — pure documentation, no code or behavior change.
**Description:** Two edits. **(1)** `docs/plans/class-content-status.md` Druid section — the Wild Shape entry was outdated ("the glue is missing") because it was written before v2.14.4 shipped the harness coverage + the v2.14.5 chip integration + the v2.14.6 over-budget gate. Updated to mark those pieces ✅ shipped. Adds a "Token swap on transform — TODO" sub-section that enumerates the remaining work: enumerate Token rows by character_id, snapshot label/image_url/size into `sheet["prior_form"]["tokens"]`, write the beast's values, broadcast `token_update` per touched token; revert restores from the snapshot. Lists four edge cases (multiple tokens per character, summons sharing char_id, custom portrait overrides, GM-spawned doubles) so a future implementer doesn't rediscover them. **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` docstring — adds a "TODO (filed in docs/plans/class-content-status.md under Druid Wild Shape): swap the character's Token row(s) to reflect the new form" callout pointing back at the plan doc. The TODO lives in the docstring (not as a comment) so it's surfaced by IDE hovers + `python -c "from ... import transform_character; help(transform_character)"`.
**Description (cont):** Why a TODO instead of just doing the work. The token-swap implementation has at least four edge-case branches (multiple tokens, summons, custom portraits, NPC clones) that each need a design decision. Doing it now without a triggering use-case (Mira's Lv 5 Wild Shape mostly produces Wolf-sized beasts, which look fine as-is) would be speculative work — the canvas-rendering investment is non-trivial relative to the gameplay payoff for a Lv 5 Moon Druid. Filing the TODO with the full checklist means whoever picks it up (could be the next Phase B commit, could be a future fix-this-when-it-actually-matters pass) has a complete picture without rediscovering the surface area.
**Description (cont 2):** Why update the existing plan-doc entry instead of just appending a note. The plan-doc entry for Wild Shape was wrong about the current state — it claimed "the glue is missing" when the glue had shipped in v2.14.4-v2.14.6. Leaving the wrong claim in place would mislead future maintainers (myself included) into thinking the integration work was still open. Replacing the stale text + appending the new TODO is more honest about what's done vs filed.

Neither is on the current per-feature roadmap.

## [2.15.7] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.7 — Cutting Words (Lore Bard Lv 3).** Ships the reaction that spends one Bardic Inspiration use to subtract a BI die roll from an enemy attack roll, ability check, or damage roll. Dedicated `/api/campaign/{cid}/use_cutting_words` endpoint mirrors the `/use_bardic_inspiration` shape: validates Lore Bard ≥ Lv 3, decrements the BI counter, rolls the BI die server-side (d6/d8/d10/d12 by Bard level, same table), marks the reaction slot, broadcasts a `feature_used` roll-log card with the rolled value, broadcasts `resource_update` for the BI decrement, and returns the rolled value + remaining uses. Class-feature button on Lyra's sheet (added to `_bard_sheet`'s `class_features` array) routes to the new endpoint. No roll-time intercept yet (the plan doc's (B) infrastructure) — the GM applies the subtraction manually to whatever roll triggered the reaction; the announce attributes the source. PATCH — additive endpoint, no schema change, no breaking change to existing endpoints.
**Description:** Five coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new `/api/campaign/{campaign_id}/use_cutting_words` endpoint (~120 lines, mirror of `/use_bardic_inspiration`). Validates Lore Bard subclass (single-class OR any multi-class entry with subclass containing "lore") and Bard level ≥ 3 — both return 409 with detail strings if violated (defense-in-depth; client filters the button visibility). Validates BI resource present (404) and non-zero (409 `out_of_uses`). Phase 4 over-budget gate on the reaction slot (409 `error:"over_budget", slot:"reaction", source:"cutting-words"`). Die size derives from `_bard_level_from_sheet` (already shared by /use_bardic_inspiration and /rest's Song of Rest). Server rolls 1d{N} via `dice_mod.roll`, decrements the BI counter, marks the reaction slot via `_mark_battle_economy`, broadcasts `feature_used` with the rolled value ("🎭 Cutting Words → -3 from {target}'s roll" or "from a creature's roll" when no target was supplied), broadcasts `resource_update` for bardic-inspiration. Returns `{ok, die, rolled, breakdown, target_id, target_name, remaining, over_budget}`. The `target_character_id` body field is optional — when omitted (e.g. an NPC enemy that's in the encounter's tokens but not the Character table), the announce reads generically. **(2)** `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` Bard block — adds `'cutting-words'` (reaction, class:'bard', subclass:'lore', unlock_level:3, descriptive label + desc). The curated entry's `subclass:'lore'` field follows the v2.14.3 pattern. **(3)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` mirror — adds `"cutting-words": {"slot": "reaction"}`. **(4)** `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet` — Lyra grows a `class_features` array (previously absent) with a single Cutting Words entry. Lyra is the demo's only Lore Bard ≥ Lv 3, so she's the only PC where the button renders. Other PCs' `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['cutting-words']` lookups also return the curated entry but the Class abilities panel only renders entries present on their own sheet's `class_features` array, so no cross-character bleed. **(5)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — the existing `.cf-use` click handler grows a `isCuttingWords` branch: when the button's `data-feature === 'cutting-words'`, the fetch goes to `/use_cutting_words` instead of `/use_feature` and the body is the cutting-words shape (`character_id` + `override` only — no `feature_key` etc.). 409 handling extends to recognize `error:"out_of_uses"` as a non-modal early-return. Success toast specializes: "🎭 Cutting Words: rolled -{N}{from target} (reaction)". The other features' code path is untouched.
**Description (cont):** Harness — new `tests/harness/test_use_cutting_words.py` file with 6 tests: happy path (Lyra cuts Pip → d8 die, rolled value in 1..8, target_name=Pip, decrement, feature_used + resource_update broadcasts assert on the rolled value text), no-target variant (omit target_character_id → response carries target_name=None, broadcast reads "from a creature's roll"), 400 missing character_id, 404 unknown character, 409 wrong-class (Pip is a Rogue → eligibility gate 409), 409 out-of-uses (drain BI then attempt — drain-loop pattern from v2.15.3). The new `lyra_full_bi` fixture long-rests Lyra at test setup so each test has a deterministic BI baseline. **One auxiliary test fix lands here too:** `tests/harness/test_use_bardic_inspiration.py::test_bi_happy_path` gained a pre-flight long-rest because the cutting-words tests (alphabetically earlier — `test_use_cutting_words.py` < `test_use_bardic_inspiration.py`... actually `c` < `b` is false; `b` < `c` so BI runs FIRST alphabetically — wait let me reread). Pytest order is file-collection order within a directory; the test_use_bardic_inspiration.py file runs before test_use_cutting_words.py alphabetically (b < c). So actually the cutting-words tests run AFTER the BI test in a normal suite run. The failure I hit was due to the cutting-words tests' fixture refilling Lyra to 3/3 BI but then the out_of_uses test depleting her to 0/3, leaving the suite session in that state for any subsequent rerun. The BI happy-path test's pre-flight long-rest makes it order-independent regardless. Suite grows 64 → 70 tests.
**Description (cont 2):** Why no target picker. RAW Cutting Words: "When a creature that you can see within 60 feet of you makes an attack roll, an ability check, or a damage roll, you can use your reaction." The target is the creature whose roll is being cut. Today's UI doesn't have a roll-time intercept (the plan doc files this under infrastructure piece (B) — "subscribe to attack / save / damage broadcasts and offer a reaction prompt to eligible characters"). So Cutting Words ships as a manual button the bard clicks AFTER hearing an enemy's roll. The target_character_id field on the endpoint is supplied when the bard knows exactly which PC/NPC they're cutting (and that PC/NPC has a Character row); omitted when the target is an NPC in the encounter's tokens only. A future commit that implements the reaction-prompt UI would change this from a manual button to a triggered prompt + target prefilled from the triggering roll's source.
**Description (cont 3):** Why a new endpoint instead of extending /use_bardic_inspiration. The shapes are similar (both decrement BI + announce) but the differences add up: Cutting Words rolls the die server-side AND embeds the rolled value in the broadcast (BI doesn't roll — the recipient ally adds the die to a future roll); Cutting Words marks the REACTION slot (BI marks BONUS); Cutting Words doesn't enforce "not self" (the bard can technically cut their own roll... wait actually RAW says "a creature you can see" which doesn't exclude self, but practically a bard doesn't cut themselves — this is moot UI-wise since the bard wouldn't click the button for their own roll); Cutting Words requires Lore subclass + Lv 3+ (BI is at-will for any Bard ≥ Lv 1). Extending the existing endpoint with a `mode: "inspire" | "cut"` parameter would have spread the differences across both code paths and made each branch harder to read. A dedicated endpoint with a parallel shape is more maintainable. The mirror is intentional: future shared changes (e.g. swap dice_mod for a deterministic test harness die) update both endpoints' bodies identically.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open `/campaign/1` as the GM. Open Lyra's full sheet. The Class abilities panel (between Inventory and the spell list per the sheet's layout) now shows "Cutting Words" as a clickable row. Click "Use" → /use_cutting_words fires, BI counter drops 3→2, Lyra's Reaction chip on the init mini-sheet flips on, the roll-log card reads "🎭 Cutting Words → -{N} from a creature's roll" (no target picker yet; N is the actual d8 roll Lyra got), the local toast confirms "🎭 Cutting Words: rolled -{N} (reaction)". Click again → 2→1, reaction chip stays burnt (idempotent). Click two more times to fully deplete BI → 4th click toasts "Bardic Inspiration (Cutting Words): no uses remaining" without firing the endpoint. Test the over-budget gate by burning Lyra's reaction chip first via... well, today the only way for the bard's reaction chip to be marked is Cutting Words itself, so the gate only fires on the second click in the same round (the 1st marks reaction; the 2nd hits the gate). The Phase 4 modal asks "Lyra've already used your reaction this turn. Roll Cutting Words anyway?" — Confirm re-fires with override:true, Cancel preserves both chip and BI counter.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/api/campaign/{campaign_id}/use_cutting_words` — new endpoint mirroring `/use_bardic_inspiration` for the Lore Bard Lv 3 reaction.
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` `'cutting-words'` entry — slot:reaction, class:bard, subclass:lore, unlock_level:3.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` `"cutting-words": {"slot": "reaction"}` mirror entry.
- `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet` `class_features` — new array with the Cutting Words entry so Lyra's Class abilities panel renders the button.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `.cf-use` click handler — `isCuttingWords` branch that routes to `/use_cutting_words` with the cutting-words body shape + specialized success toast.
- `tests/harness/test_use_cutting_words.py` — new file with 6 tests: happy path, no-target variant, 400 missing fields, 404 unknown character, 409 wrong-class, 409 out-of-uses drain-loop.

### Changed
- `tests/harness/test_use_bardic_inspiration.py::test_bi_happy_path` — adds a pre-flight long-rest + `gm_ws.mark()` so the test is order-independent regardless of which earlier tests depleted Lyra's BI counter.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Lyra's full sheet as the GM. Class abilities panel shows "Cutting Words". Click Use → BI counter 3→2, reaction chip on init mini-sheet flips, roll-log card "🎭 Cutting Words → -{N} from a creature's roll" with {N} being the actual d8 roll. The GM mentally subtracts {N} from whatever NPC roll just happened. Click 2 more times to drain BI; the 4th click toasts "no uses remaining". Burn reaction first (via some other reaction-class feature — today there isn't one, so the gate only triggers on the 2nd-in-same-round click). The over-budget modal fires with cutting-words-flavoured copy.
- **Why Lyra's Cutting Words isn't tied to her resource counter via the resource ⚡ Use button.** The Bardic Inspiration resource counter ⚡ Use opens the BI target-picker (v2.11.0) — the picker is hardcoded to the "inspire an ally" flow. Cutting Words consumes the same BI counter but it's a different ACTION. Putting it on a class_features button (the Class abilities panel) cleanly separates the two flows: the resource ⚡ Use is for inspiring; the Cutting Words button is for cutting. Both decrement the same BI counter.
- **Where the target picker would go.** A future commit could add a target picker that shows enemies / all combatants from the encounter token list (not the roster, since NPCs without Character rows aren't in roster). The picker would route through `showTargetPicker` from `resource_option_picker.js` — same primitive Bardic Inspiration uses. Filed as B.7.1 along with the roll-time intercept (B) infrastructure.
- **WSCollector mark() pattern.** The test file uses `gm_ws.mark()` after the `lyra_full_bi` fixture's long-rest to discard the rest's broadcasts (which themselves include a `resource_update` for bardic-inspiration when the fixture refills the counter). Without the mark, the test's post-cut `wait_for("resource_update")` would latch onto the rest's refill (current=3) instead of the cut's decrement (current=2). This pattern is established for any test that uses a fixture which itself broadcasts on a type the test asserts on.
- **Cutting Words tests can't run in parallel with each other due to suite-shared state.** Each test drains Lyra's BI counter; the fixture refills via long-rest before the next test runs. pytest's default sequential per-file execution handles this fine. A future parallel-test setup would need per-test character isolation (e.g. a fixture campaign with one Lyra per worker).
- **Phase B class-content content audit.** With B.1 (CD Devotion) + B.2 (Wild Shape harness + chip + over-budget) + B.3 (Magical Secrets) + B.3.1 (Lyra Lv 6) + B.4 (Jack of All Trades) + B.5 (Song of Rest + Short/Long Rest UI routing) + B.6 (Divine Sense) + B.7 (this commit, Cutting Words) all shipped, Lyra's College of Lore feature set is complete (Cutting Words + Additional Magical Secrets at Lv 6 — Peerless Skill unlocks at Lv 14 and is filed for the future Lv 14+ fixture). The remaining Phase B candidates are non-Lyra: Caelan's Lay on Hands (already shipped v2.10.0), Channel Divinity Devotion (v2.14.3), Divine Sense (v2.15.6); Mira's Wild Shape (v2.14.4 + v2.14.5 + v2.14.6); Tavik's Channel Divinity (v2.9.0); Pip's Cunning Action (v2.6.0). Phase B feels mostly wrapped for the current Lv 5-6 demo party.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.7.1) Cutting Words target picker — show enemies / encounter combatants. (B.8) Remarkable Athlete pattern — needs a Fighter Champion fixture. (B.9) Divine Smite uplift — extend the attack-roll picker to offer "+ Divine Smite (spend L1+ slot)". (B.10) Peerless Skill (Lore Bard Lv 14) — needs a Lv 14+ Bard fixture. (B.11) Roll-time intercept (B) infrastructure — would unlock real-reaction prompts for Cutting Words, Shield, Counterspell, Hellish Rebuke, and several other reaction features. Bigger lift; would touch the WS event bus shape.

## [2.15.6] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.6 — Divine Sense announce + Cleansing Touch curated entry.** Adds Paladin Lv 1's Divine Sense as an announce-only action that fires from Caelan's resource ⚡ Use button: POSTs `/use_feature` for the roll-log card + chip flip + over-budget gate, then decrements the existing 4/4 resource counter. Adds the Cleansing Touch (Paladin Lv 14) entry to the curated `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` tables for forward compat — no demo PC has it (Caelan is Lv 5) but the server now accepts the slug so a future Lv 14+ Paladin fixture can fire it without a code change. PATCH — additive feature curation + UI wiring, no schema change.
**Description:** Four coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` Paladin block — adds `'divine-sense'` (action, paladin, unlock_level: 1, "Detect celestial / fiend / undead within 60 ft until end of next turn. 1 + CHA mod uses per long rest.") and `'cleansing-touch'` (action, paladin, unlock_level: 14, "End one spell on yourself or one willing creature you touch. CHA mod uses per long rest."). The Cleansing Touch entry carries a comment noting that the RAW target-picker UI is filed for a future Lv 14+ fixture commit; today /use_feature accepts the key generically without a target. **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` mirror — adds the matching `"divine-sense": {"slot": "action"}` and `"cleansing-touch": {"slot": "action"}` entries. **(3)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` resource ⚡ Use click handler — new special-case branch BEFORE the Wild Shape branch: when `k === 'divine-sense'`, the handler pre-checks remaining uses, decrements the counter via the existing `postUse(k, -1, false)` path (no toast — /use_feature provides the broadcast), then POSTs /use_feature with `feature_key:'divine-sense'` + the curated label / desc. Standard over-budget handling: 409 with `error:"over_budget"` → `handleOverBudget` modal → retry with `override:true`. Other 409 / non-200 responses surface via `_toast`. On success a local "👁 Divine Sense announced — the GM will tell you what you sense." toast confirms the click. **(4)** `tests/harness/test_use_feature.py` — adds `test_divine_sense_announces` (Caelan POSTs /use_feature with feature_key='divine-sense'; asserts response shape + the `feature_used` WS broadcast) and `test_cleansing_touch_curated` (same shape for cleansing-touch — the table is wired even though Caelan can't legitimately use it RAW; the server doesn't enforce class-level eligibility on /use_feature). Suite grows 62 → 64 tests.
**Description (cont):** Why Divine Sense is special-cased instead of generalized. The resource ⚡ Use handler already has several feature-specific branches (channel-divinity v2.9.0 picker, lay-on-hands v2.10.0 amount + target picker, bardic-inspiration v2.11.0 target picker, wild-shape v2.14.4 BeastPicker). Each has bespoke UX. Divine Sense is the simplest of the bunch — no picker, no target, just announce + decrement — but it still warranted its own branch because the default ⚡ Use path (just `postUse(-1, true)` to /resource) doesn't broadcast a `feature_used` WS message, doesn't mark the action-economy chip, doesn't fire the over-budget gate. Generalizing this into "any resource whose key is in `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` auto-routes through /use_feature" would be a Phase B refactor (touches every PC sheet's resource list), worth doing once a few more features land on the same shape. Today's two announce-only features (Divine Sense + the future Cleansing Touch) are too few for the refactor to be worth it.
**Description (cont 2):** Why Cleansing Touch is in the curated tables but has no UI branch. Caelan is Lv 5 and Cleansing Touch unlocks at Lv 14 (RAW); the demo can't exercise it today. Adding the table entry is cheap (six lines across JS + Python) and forward-compatible — when a Lv 14+ Paladin demo PC lands (Phase A.7+?), the resource counter on their sheet plus a future "target-picker UI" commit will be the only remaining work. The harness test `test_cleansing_touch_curated` is the contract pin: the table entry resolves to slot=action and /use_feature broadcasts the right `feature_used` shape. If a future commit accidentally removes the table entry, this test fails before merge.
**Description (cont 3):** Server-side enforcement of class-level eligibility — explicitly NOT done. /use_feature doesn't check that the caster's class+level qualifies for the feature (no "you must be a Paladin ≥ Lv 14 to cast Cleansing Touch" gate). The client's resource panel naturally hides features the PC doesn't have on their sheet, so the gate is implicit at the sheet layer. A determined player using the API directly could fire `feature_key:'cleansing-touch'` from a Lv 1 Rogue — the server would broadcast `feature_used` and mark the action chip. That's accepted today as the trust model (player-side homebrew / GM-fudging is a feature, not a bug). If we later need server-side eligibility checks (e.g. to block cheats in tournament play), the curated table can grow a `min_level: 14` field that /use_feature reads against the caster's sheet — a small future commit.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open Caelan's full sheet at `/campaign/1`. Class Resources panel shows three counters: Lay on Hands 25/25, Divine Sense 4/4, Channel Divinity 1/1. Click ⚡ Use on the Divine Sense row → new path fires: Caelan's Act chip flips on the init mini-sheet (Divine Sense is an action), the roll-log card shows "🛡 Divine Sense" with the curated description, the counter drops to 3/4, the local toast confirms "👁 Divine Sense announced — the GM will tell you what you sense." Click ⚡ Use four more times → on the 5th click the counter is at 0 and the path returns early with a "Divine Sense: no uses remaining" toast. Click ⚡ Use after Caelan's Act chip is already burnt → the v2.6.1 over-budget modal fires ("Caelan've already used your action this turn. Roll Divine Sense anyway?"); Confirm re-POSTs with `override:true` and the use lands; Cancel leaves both the counter and the chip alone.

### Added
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — `'divine-sense'` + `'cleansing-touch'` entries in the Paladin block.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — `"divine-sense"` + `"cleansing-touch"` entries in the Python mirror dict.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` resource ⚡ Use handler — `k === 'divine-sense'` special-case that decrements counter + POSTs /use_feature with over-budget retry.
- `tests/harness/test_use_feature.py::test_divine_sense_announces` — happy-path test for Caelan's Divine Sense.
- `tests/harness/test_use_feature.py::test_cleansing_touch_curated` — forward-compat contract pin for Cleansing Touch.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Caelan's full sheet. Class Resources panel → click ⚡ on Divine Sense. Roll-log card "🛡 Divine Sense", counter 4→3, Act chip on init tracker flips. Click 4 more times to deplete the counter; the 5th click toasts "no uses remaining" without firing /use_feature. Burn Caelan's Act chip via some other action (e.g. cast Bless), then click ⚡ Divine Sense → the over-budget modal appears; Confirm re-fires with override; Cancel preserves both counter and chip. Tavik's Channel Divinity (Cleric) and Lyra's Bardic Inspiration paths are unchanged.

- **Cleansing Touch is deferred for full UX.** Curated table entry ships; the resource counter + target-picker UI requires (a) a Lv 14+ Paladin demo PC (filed under Phase A.7), (b) a target-picker that handles "willing creature you touch" (reuse the lay-on-hands picker from v2.10.0). Both are small follow-ups; not bundled here to keep the commit focused on Divine Sense.

## [2.15.5] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.5.2 — route the sheet's 💤 Long Rest button through `/rest`.** Closes the second gap filed at the end of v2.15.4: Long Rest was still rolling its own local state mutations and asking the player to remember to Save, which meant the server's resource_update / spell_slot_update broadcasts never fired and the persistence step was a manual save click. Now both rest buttons go through `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/rest` and persist server-side in one network call. Adds `test_long_rest_happy_path` to `tests/harness/test_rest.py` for the response-shape contract. PATCH — UI wiring + new test; no schema change.
**Description:** Two changes. **(1)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `long-rest-btn` handler — pre-v2.15.5 was a synchronous block that mutated `hpInp.value = hpMax`, `tempInp.value = 0`, `hdInp.value = min(hdMax, hdCur + max(1, hdMax//2))`, walked `.ss-row` elements to reset their `data-used` to 0, called `window._mcRefillResources('long')` for the Class Resources panel, and toasted "Save to persist." The new handler keeps the same UX (confirm dialog up front), then POSTs `/rest` with `{type:"long"}`, syncs `hpInp` / `tempInp` / `hdInp` from `data.hp` + `data.hit_dice`, still resets spell-slot pips + calls `_mcRefillResources('long')` for in-page sync (the server's WS broadcasts arrive later; doing both means the sheet feels instant). Standalone fallback (no CAMPAIGN_ID / no char_id) preserves the legacy local-only path so the standalone character-editor page outside a campaign keeps working. **(2)** `tests/harness/test_rest.py` `test_long_rest_happy_path` — new test. POSTs `/rest` with `type=long` for Pip and asserts the response shape: `ok:true`, `type:"long"`, `hp.current == hp.max` (full restore), `hp.temp == 0` (cleared), `hit_dice.current >= 1` + `<= max` (partial recovery per RAW), `resources` field present (may be empty for Pip; the Rogue has no short/long counters in the demo seed). Suite grows 61 → 62 tests.
**Description (cont):** Why no spell-slot assertion in the test. The /rest endpoint resets `sheet["spell_slots"][cls][lvl].used = 0` for every class/level, but the response payload doesn't carry the slot map — it only broadcasts `spell_slot_update` WS messages per slot level. The test would have to subscribe to the campaign WS and assert on the broadcasts, which is doable but adds setup the other test cases don't need. The harness already covers spell-slot WS broadcasts indirectly via `test_cast_spell.py`, and the slot reset on long rest is a 5-line server-side operation that's hard to silently regress without breaking the response's `ok:true`. Filed as a potential v2.x harness add if it ever matters; the contract-shape coverage of this commit is enough for the v2.15.5 wiring.
**Description (cont 2):** Why keep the local resource-refill + spell-slot-pip reset code. The server-routed flow mutates the database and broadcasts the WS messages, but the sheet page may not be WS-subscribed (the spell-slot WS handler listens via `vtt:ws-message` in some contexts but the standalone character editor doesn't open a WS at all). The local resets do in-page sync immediately; the WS broadcasts handle the OTHER connected clients (the GM's init tracker mini-sheet, a second browser window viewing the same character, etc.). Doing both is idempotent — last-write-wins on the form field, and the server's view is the source of truth on the next save / next page load. The redundancy is cheap and the alternative (subscribe the sheet to WS exclusively, drop the local resets) would require deeper plumbing changes.
**Description (cont 3):** Why no `/roll` follow-up like Short Rest. Long Rest doesn't surface a recovery roll — HP just snaps to max, no dice rolled. There's no analogous "Pip took a long rest: 1d8+1d6+1 = 9" log entry to mirror. A "rest summary" announce (something like "💤 Pip Quickfingers took a long rest. HP restored to 33/33, hit dice 4/5, spell slots refilled.") would be useful but requires a new broadcast type, not just a /roll POST. Filed as part of the same future `rest_summary` WS broadcast idea from v2.15.4.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open Pip's full sheet at `/campaign/1`. Damage him to e.g. 5/33 HP, spend a HD (now 4/5 HD), cast Sneak Attack a few times. Click 💤 Long Rest → confirm dialog → toast "💤 Long rest complete". HP input snaps to 33. Temp HP input snaps to 0. HD input goes to min(5, 4+2)=5 (full restore in this case because RAW long rest restores half max HD, and 5//2=2 covers the 1 HD he spent). The Class Resources panel auto-refills any short/long-rest counters. No "Save the sheet" reminder needed — the server already persisted. Switch to Lyra → her Bardic Inspiration counter resets to 3/3 (short-rest refresh) and her spell slots all show as available again. Bonus check: open Pip's sheet in a second browser window (or from the GM side) — the second window auto-refreshes from the `resource_update` + `spell_slot_update` broadcasts even though it wasn't the one that clicked the button.

### Added
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `long-rest-btn` handler — POSTs to `/rest` with type=long, consumes the response, and falls back to the legacy local path for standalone-sheet contexts.
- `tests/harness/test_rest.py::test_long_rest_happy_path` — happy-path test for the long-rest contract shape.

### Changed
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `long-rest-btn` handler — server is now the source of truth for HP / HD / spell slots / resources. The "Save to persist" reminder is gone; the rest is persisted by the time the click handler returns.
- Confirm-dialog body trimmed — no longer says "Remember to Save the sheet afterward" since the /rest call persists synchronously.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, log in as GM. Damage Pip a bit (decrement HP via the - HP button on his init mini-sheet) and spend some hit dice via Short Rest a couple times. Click 💤 Long Rest on Pip's full sheet. HP / Temp HP / HD inputs all update from the server response; the toast confirms the rest. Open the Class Resources panel on a PC with refillable counters (Lyra's Bardic Inspiration, Caelan's Channel Divinity, Mira's Wild Shape) — those counters refill via the resource_update broadcast.
- **Standalone sheet page.** Same as Short Rest: opening `/character/{id}/sheet` outside a campaign falls back to the legacy local-only path. HP / HD / spell-slot inputs mutate in-page; the sheet still asks for a manual Save in that context (well, the toast doesn't say so but the data is local-only until you click Save).
- **No new endpoint.** The /rest endpoint's long-rest branch existed pre-2.0.0; only the UI handler is new. The harness test added in this commit pins down the long-rest response shape that the v2.15.5 sheet handler reads.
- **Phase B.5 is now fully wrapped.** B.5 (Song of Rest server-side) + B.5.1 (Short Rest UI routing) + B.5.2 (this — Long Rest UI routing). Both rest buttons now go through the same server endpoint; the server is the source of truth; the demo flows end-to-end without manual saves.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. (B.7) Cutting Words — Lyra's reaction-on-hit reduction (Lore Bard Lv 3 feature, needs a reaction-prompt UI surface that doesn't exist yet — bigger lift than the others).
