## [2.15.4] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.5.1 — route the sheet's ⚡ Short Rest button through `/rest`.** Closes the gap filed at the end of v2.15.3: the sheet's Short Rest button rolled locally with `Math.random()` and posted a separate `/roll` for the campaign log, which meant the v2.15.3 Song of Rest bonus + the resource-refill broadcasts + the death-save state machine NEVER fired from the UI — only direct `/rest` callers (today: harness tests, tomorrow: any future batch-rest modal) benefitted. Now the button POSTs to `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/rest` and uses the server's response to update HP / HD / roll toast. The campaign log entry still goes through `/roll` (different roll values, but the note now reads "⚡ Short rest hit die + 🪄 Song of Rest (Lyra Sunstrider)" so other players see the attribution). PATCH — UI wiring tweak on an existing endpoint, no schema change, no API contract change.
**Description:** One file changed: `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html`. The short-rest click handler at line 5442 was a ~65-line block that read HD/CON from form fields, rolled `1 + Math.floor(Math.random() * dieSize)` locally, mutated `hpInp.value` + `hdInp.value` directly, fired a roll toast with the local breakdown, then posted to `/roll` with the same expression (server re-rolled for the log entry, accepting the divergence between toast and log). The new handler does: **(1)** Pre-check `hdInp.value > 0` (unchanged early-out for "no HD left"); **(2)** Fall through to the OLD local path when there's no CAMPAIGN_ID or no char_id (standalone sheet edit page outside a campaign — keeps the button functional in that legacy context); **(3)** POST `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/rest` with `{type:"short"}`; **(4)** Handle 409 `no_hit_dice` (form-state-out-of-sync edge case) + generic non-200 errors via `_toast`; **(5)** Sync `hpInp.value` ← `data.hp.current` and `hdInp.value` ← `data.hit_dice.current` (form fields stay aligned with the server's view without needing a save round-trip); **(6)** Build `note` with Song of Rest attribution when `data.song_of_rest != null` ("⚡ Short rest hit die + 🪄 Song of Rest (Lyra Sunstrider)"); **(7)** Fire the roll toast with `data.expression` + `data.breakdown` + `data.recovered` straight from the server response (so Song of Rest's `+1d6` term + its rolled value both show in the toast verbatim); **(8)** Post to `/roll` for the campaign log with the SERVER's `data.expression` (so the log entry's dice expression matches what the player rolled in shape, even if the numbers differ because /roll re-rolls — the `note` field carries the actual server roll result for context).
**Description (cont):** Why the standalone fallback path stays. The button can be clicked from a few different contexts: the in-campaign full sheet (CAMPAIGN_ID + char_id both available), the standalone character-editor page (`/character/{id}/sheet` — no CAMPAIGN_ID), and a hypothetical future read-only preview. The `/rest` endpoint requires both `campaign_id` and `char_id` in the URL — calling it from a standalone page would 403 ("Not a member"). The fallback preserves the local-roll behavior so the standalone page's Short Rest button stays functional. The downside: standalone short rests still don't get Song of Rest. That's accurate to the demo's design — the standalone page is for sheet editing, not active play; party-context features like Song of Rest belong to in-campaign play.
**Description (cont 2):** Why the campaign-log /roll still fires even though /rest already mutated state. The /rest endpoint emits two WS broadcast types: `character_death_save` (only when HP / dying-state changes) and `resource_update` (for refilled short-rest counters). Neither posts a generic "rest happened" entry to the campaign roll log — and the log is what other players + the GM see in the chat. Without the follow-up /roll, the GM watching the chat would see Pip's resource counters refresh but no announcement of the rest itself. The /roll post is a fire-and-forget log entry; the divergence between its rolled values and the actual rest's recovery is pre-existing behavior (the comment in the v2.15.3 code already called this out). A future commit could add a `rest_summary` WS broadcast to /rest that the log subscribes to, eliminating the divergence — filed as a backlog idea.
**Description (cont 3):** Why no new harness test. The harness already covers /rest's server contract (test_short_rest_song_of_rest_happy_path in v2.15.3 asserts the response shape). The new code is the JS handler that calls /rest — exercising it requires a browser, which is the Playwright UI layer's job. The existing `test_short_rest_song_of_rest_happy_path` would have caught a regression in /rest itself; the JS handler change is a thin wrapper that just posts the standard payload. Suite stays at 61 tests.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open Pip's full sheet at `/campaign/1` as the GM (or as Alice if she has access via her player account). Click ⚡ Short Rest. The roll toast now reads `1d8[X]+1d6[Y]+1 => Z` (the +1d6 is Lyra's Song of Rest, server-rolled) instead of the pre-v2.15.4 `1d8[X]+1 => Z`. The Pip's HP and HD form fields update in place. The chat log shows a new entry "⚡ Short rest hit die + 🪄 Song of Rest (Lyra Sunstrider)" with the server's re-rolled value (one's job is the toast, the other's job is the chat log; they don't need to match exactly to be useful). Tavik's mini-sheet (open the init tracker if he's mid-encounter) would auto-update his Channel Divinity counter via the `resource_update` broadcast if the rest happened to refill anything.

### Added
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `short-rest-btn` handler — POSTs to `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/rest` and consumes the response.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `short-rest-btn` handler — falls back to the local-roll path when CAMPAIGN_ID or char_id is missing (standalone character-editor page).
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `short-rest-btn` `note` string — Song of Rest attribution suffix `+ 🪄 Song of Rest ({bard name})` when applicable.

### Changed
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `short-rest-btn` handler — server is now the source of truth for HD spend, HP recovery, and Song of Rest bonus. Form fields sync from the response. The local `Math.random()` roll is now the fallback for the standalone-sheet context only.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, log in as GM. Open Pip's full sheet. Click ⚡ Short Rest. Toast reads `1d8[X]+1d6[Y]+1 => Z` (the +1d6 is the new Song of Rest term). HP / HD inputs update. Chat log shows the new note with the Bard attribution. Open Lyra's sheet — her own short rest also includes the +1d6 (RAW: "you or any friendly creatures who can hear your performance"). Open a non-Bard's PC (e.g. Caelan) and short-rest — same +1d6 bonus because Lyra is in the campaign. Switch to a fresh campaign with no Bard — the rest reads `1d10[X]+CON = Y` with no bonus term.
- **Standalone sheet page.** Open `/character/{id}/sheet` (no campaign context). Click Short Rest — falls through to the local Math.random() path, no Song of Rest, no campaign log entry. Form fields still update.
- **Why the toast and the chat log can disagree.** /rest does the actual recovery (HP + HD + resources + state changes). The follow-up /roll fires a fresh roll for the log entry — the numbers differ from the toast because /roll re-rolls. The `note` field carries the announce text ("⚡ Short rest hit die + 🪄 Song of Rest (Lyra)"), which is the part players actually read. A future commit could replace the /roll fire-and-forget with a `rest_summary` WS broadcast from /rest itself, eliminating the divergence — filed as backlog.
- **Long Rest button is unchanged.** It still goes through the legacy in-browser flow (resets HP, HD, spell slots locally) instead of POSTing /rest with type=long. Long Rest doesn't benefit from Song of Rest (RAW: Song of Rest is short-rest only), so this commit doesn't touch it. Routing Long Rest through /rest would surface the server's resource-refill broadcasts uniformly — filed as B.5.2.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.5.2) Route Long Rest through /rest type=long for the same WS-broadcast / resource-refill plumbing that this commit gives Short Rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. (B.7) Cutting Words — Lyra's reaction-on-hit reduction (Lore Bard Lv 3 feature, needs a reaction-prompt UI surface that doesn't exist yet — bigger lift than the others).

## [2.15.3] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.5 — Song of Rest.** Adds the Bard's passive +1dN healing per Hit Die spent during a short rest, scaling with the highest-level Bard in the campaign (d6 at Lv 2-8, d8 at Lv 9-12, d10 at Lv 13-16, d12 at Lv 17+). The `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/rest` endpoint now folds the bonus die into the same recovery roll expression so the existing `dice_mod.roll` call produces a single breakdown (`1d8[5]+1d6[3]+1 => 9`) instead of needing a separate rolling pass. Response gains a `song_of_rest: {die, bard, bard_level}` field for UI attribution. Immediately exercisable in the demo via Lyra (Lv 6 Lore Bard from v2.15.1) — Pip's short rest auto-includes +1d6 from her performance. PATCH — additive to an existing endpoint, no schema change, response is backward-compatible (new field can be `null`).
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — new helper `_song_of_rest_for_campaign(db, campaign_id)` at line 6147 (right below `_bard_level_from_sheet`). Queries every `Character` in the campaign, calls the existing `_bard_level_from_sheet` on each sheet, keeps the highest Bard level + that character's name. Returns `(die_size, bard_name, bard_level)` where `die_size` follows the RAW scaling table (d6/d8/d10/d12 by Bard level tier); returns `(0, "", 0)` when no eligible Bard exists. Multi-bard parties use the highest-level Bard's die (RAW: bonus dice don't stack — one bard's performance is enough). **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `rest_character` short-rest branch — after computing the class HD + CON mod, calls `_song_of_rest_for_campaign(db, campaign_id)` and appends `+1d{N}` to the dice expression when applicable. The single `dice_mod.roll` call handles both terms; the breakdown reads `"1d8[X]+1d6[Y]+Z => total"` naturally. Response payload gains `song_of_rest: {die, bard, bard_level}` (or `null` when no Bard is present) so the UI can attribute the bonus to a specific PC. **(3)** `tests/harness/test_rest.py` — new file with three tests: happy path (Pip short-rests, response carries `song_of_rest.die==6`, `song_of_rest.bard=="Lyra Sunstrider"`, expression contains `+1d6`, breakdown contains both `1d8[` and `1d6[`), invalid-type 400 (`type:"nap"`), and no-HD 409 (drain loop until 409, sanity that the new code doesn't skip the early-exit guard). Suite grows 58 → 61 tests.
**Description (cont):** Why the bonus is folded into the same expression. The cleanest alternative was rolling two separate `dice_mod.roll` calls and summing the totals, then composing a custom breakdown string. The single-expression path is shorter, reuses the established roll plumbing, and produces a breakdown that's already in the format every other roll uses (so the chat-log card and the toast both render correctly without UI changes). The minor downside: the response's `recovered` field is the combined total, not the base-vs-Song-of-Rest split — but the `breakdown` string carries both rolls individually, so the client can parse it apart if it wants to display "your HD rolled 5, Lyra's Song of Rest added 3."
**Description (cont 2):** Why query every campaign character, not just party members. The campaign's `Character` table is the closest thing to "the party" the schema models today — there's no separate `party` concept distinct from "characters in the campaign." A future commit could narrow to characters that are in the active encounter / on the active map / explicitly opted into the rest, but for the demo's "your whole party rests together" assumption, "any Bard in the campaign" is correct. RAW also says "any friendly creatures who can hear your performance" — proximity isn't currently modelled (no per-token range check), so all-PCs-in-campaign is the right approximation.
**Description (cont 3):** Why no broadcast for Song of Rest. Other Bard features (Bardic Inspiration, Magical Secrets picks) fire `feature_used` broadcasts that show up in the roll log. Song of Rest doesn't because (a) the bard isn't actively spending a feature use — there's no resource counter, no chip cost, no decrement to broadcast; (b) the short-rest result itself isn't a public broadcast (the endpoint only fires `character_death_save` if the rest triggers a status change), so adding an unsolicited `feature_used` would be noisier than the surrounding state changes. The `song_of_rest` field on the response is sufficient attribution for the UI; chat-log integration can come later if the demo session needs it.
**Description (cont 4):** Test isolation — the no-HD test uses a drain-loop pattern. RAW long-rest restores `max(1, hd_max // 2)` HD per rest, not all of them. So if Pip starts at 0/5 HD (from a prior test run that depleted them and the demo container hasn't reset), the fixture's long-rest brings him to only 2/5 — not 5/5. A hardcoded `for _ in range(5)` short-rest loop would fail because Pip would hit 0 HD after 2 calls, not 5. The drain-loop fix: short-rest in a `for _ in range(max_attempts)` until the call returns 409, with the 409 itself being the assertion; the safety bound stops a runaway if the no-HD guard regresses. This pattern generalises to any test that needs to deplete a resource and assert the post-depletion behavior.
**Description (cont 5):** Demo verification path. Open Pip's full sheet at `/campaign/1`. Spend a HD via the ⚡ Short Rest button. The roll log card shows the recovery roll with both dice: `Short Rest — 1d8[5]+1d6[3]+1 => 9`. The +1d6 is Lyra's Song of Rest. Open Lyra's sheet — same flow, the +1d6 still applies (RAW: "you or any friendly creatures who can hear your performance" includes the Bard themselves). Switch Lyra to a non-Bard class via the sheet editor (or open a different campaign without a Bard) — Pip's short rest now reads `1d8[5]+1 => 6` with no bonus die. The `song_of_rest` field on the response is `null` in that case.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_song_of_rest_for_campaign(db, campaign_id)` — new helper returning `(die_size, bard_name, bard_level)` per the RAW scaling table.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `rest_character` short-rest branch — folds `+1d{N}` into the recovery dice expression when an eligible Bard is in the campaign.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `rest_character` response — new `song_of_rest: {die, bard, bard_level}` field (null when no Bard ≥ Lv 2 is present).
- `tests/harness/test_rest.py` — new file with three tests covering Song of Rest happy path, invalid-type 400, and no-HD 409 drain-loop.

### Changed
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `rest_character` short-rest expression — formerly `1d{HD}{±CON}`, now `1d{HD}{+1d{N} Song of Rest}{±CON}` when a Bard ≥ Lv 2 is in the campaign. Breakdown reads `"1d8[X]+1d6[Y]+Z => total"`.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Pip's sheet. Click ⚡ Short Rest (or click "Spend HD" on the mini-sheet if your build surfaces it there). The recovery roll now includes a `+1d6` term because Lyra (Lv 6 Lore Bard) is in the campaign. POST the same call from any other PC — they all get the bonus. Open Pip again and the rest response's `song_of_rest.bard` reads "Lyra Sunstrider".
- **Why no UI tweak this commit.** The short-rest UI button at `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html:207` calls the existing client-side roll path (line 5442) which rolls locally + POSTs to `/api/.../roll` — it does NOT call the `/rest` endpoint. The `/rest` endpoint is wired but no UI calls it from the sheet today (only the v0.x batch-rest UI on the campaign settings page does). So this commit's behavior change is visible only via the harness or any caller that goes through `/rest` directly. A follow-up could route the sheet's ⚡ Short Rest button through `/rest` (would also pick up the resource refill for free) — filed as a future micro-commit.
- **Why no `feature_used` broadcast.** Song of Rest is passive — the bard doesn't spend a resource or burn an action. Other Bard features (Bardic Inspiration, Magical Secrets picks) fire `feature_used` because they DO consume something visible. Song of Rest fires silently and the response carries enough info for the UI to surface the attribution. If a future "rest summary modal" wants a richer feed, it can synthesize the announcement from the response payload.
- **Multi-bard parties.** RAW: "If you or any friendly creatures who can hear your performance regain hit points at the end of the short rest by spending one or more Hit Dice, each of those creatures regains an extra 1d6 hit points." Two bards don't stack — one is enough. `_song_of_rest_for_campaign` returns only the highest-level Bard, so the die size scales correctly but the bonus is rolled once per HD spent.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.5.1) route the sheet's ⚡ Short Rest button through `/rest` so the UI picks up Song of Rest (today only direct `/rest` callers benefit). (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. (B.7) Cutting Words — Lyra's reaction-on-hit reduction (Lore Bard Lv 3 feature, needs a reaction-prompt UI surface that doesn't exist yet — bigger lift than the others).

## [2.15.2] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.4 — Jack of All Trades (Bard Lv 2).** Adds half-PB (rounded down) to ability checks the Bard is NOT proficient in — both raw ability checks (e.g. clicking the STR card on the sheet) and non-proficient skill rolls. Applies to any sheet where the multi-class roster has a Bard entry at level ≥ 2; immediately exercisable in the demo via Lyra (Lv 6 Lore Bard from v2.15.1). Implementation: Jinja-side bonus calc updated in the skill card render, JS-side `_hasJackOfAllTrades()` helper added to `sheet.js` and wired into both the `rollAbility` and `rollSkill` click handlers. The roll's note string is suffixed with `(Jack +N)` so the toast / roll log clearly attributes the bonus. PATCH — additive behavior change to the existing roll path, no schema change, no endpoint contract change.
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — adds a Jinja `namespace`-backed `_bard_lv_ns` walker just before the skill card loop that scans both single-class (`sheet.class`) and multi-class (`sheet.classes`) entries for the highest Bard level. Derives `has_jack = _bard_lv_ns.v >= 2` + `jack_bonus = floor(PB/2) if has_jack else 0`. The per-skill bonus calc gains a third branch: previously `(prof * 2 if is_exp else (prof if is_prof else 0))`; now `(prof * 2 if is_exp else (prof if is_prof else jack_bonus))`. The card's `title=` tooltip gains `" (+{{ jack_bonus }} Jack of All Trades)"` when Jack applies, so hover surfaces the source of the extra +1. The displayed `+X` mod on the skill card reflects the Jack-modified total directly — what you see is what you roll. **(2)** `app/static/sheet.js` — new `_hasJackOfAllTrades(form)` helper above `wireDnd5eRollButtons()`. Walks `window._mcRoster()` (multi-class source-of-truth from #classes-data) for any class entry where `class === "bard" && level >= 2`; falls back to reading single-class form fields `[name="class"]` + `[name="level"]` for sheets without multi-class wiring. Inside `wireDnd5eRollButtons` the loop pre-computes `jack = _hasJackOfAllTrades(form) ? Math.floor(prof / 2) : 0`. The `rollAbility` branch adds `jack` unconditionally (RAW: raw ability checks never include PB, so Jack always applies); the `rollSkill` branch adds `jack` only when `!isExp && !isProf` (matches the template's bonus calc). Notes carry `" (Jack +N)"` in both cases so the roll-log card attributes the +1 instead of hiding it inside the d20+X total. **(3)** Saving throws are intentionally untouched. RAW: Jack of All Trades applies to "any ability check," not "any d20 roll" — saves are a distinct category that uses the same ability mod but tracks PB separately via `saving_throws.X`. A future Diviner Wizard or feat that grants half-PB to saves would need a separate hook.
**Description (cont):** Why the Jinja + JS mirror instead of one source of truth. The skill card's displayed `+X` is rendered server-side from the saved sheet state (cheap, no JS needed for read-only sheets, plays nice with the live-edit pipeline that re-syncs on save). The click handler reads from the form fields so it picks up live-edits to class/level/abilities BEFORE save — clicking a skill card right after editing CHA from 16 to 17 should roll the new value, not stale Jinja output. So both layers compute Jack, both read from the same source of truth (the form / the sheet dict), and they stay in sync because the rules are simple (`floor(PB/2)` if Bard level ≥ 2). If the Jinja computes Jack but the JS doesn't, the displayed mod would disagree with the rolled value — confusing. If only the JS computes it, the displayed mod stays stale while the roll is correct — also confusing. Both, in parallel, is the right shape.
**Description (cont 2):** Why no server-side validation. The `/api/campaign/{cid}/roll` endpoint at `tabletop_routes.py:4881` accepts an arbitrary dice expression and trusts the client to compose it correctly. Skill rolls (and all other PC rolls) go through this path with the bonus already baked in; the server doesn't re-evaluate the modifier. So Jack lives on the client side. A future commit could route skill rolls through a server-side resolver (the `_resolve_stat_modifier` helper used by `/roll_request` already exists at `tabletop_routes.py:5159` — it'd just need a Jack branch + a switch from `/roll`'s pass-through model to a resolver-based one). For today the contract is the same as expertise: client computes, server records. The 5e roll engine is too non-standard for full server-side validation anyway (advantage / disadvantage / inspiration / bardic-inspiration die / etc. all stack outside the curated table).
**Description (cont 3):** Why no harness test. `/api/campaign/{cid}/roll` accepts ANY expression and the bonus is computed client-side — there's no server-side contract to assert on. The Jack logic itself is `floor(PB / 2)` arithmetic; a unit-level test would be testing the obvious. The right test layer is Playwright UI: click Lyra's Stealth card (non-proficient, DEX 14 = +2 mod, PB 3 → Jack +1) and assert the toast reads `1d20[X]+3 => Y` not `1d20[X]+2 => Y-1`. That landing would need new harness_ui infrastructure (Lyra's sheet page interactions aren't covered by the Playwright tests yet). Filed for a future Playwright commit.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open `/campaign/1`, log in as the GM, open Lyra's sheet. Lyra is Lv 6 (v2.15.1) so Jack applies. Click any non-proficient skill card — say **Stealth** (DEX 14 → +2 ability mod, no proficiency tick) — the displayed mod is now **+3** (was +2 pre-v2.15.2), and clicking it fires a roll where the note reads `Stealth (Jack +1)`. Click **Performance** (her expertise skill: +CHA 3 + 2×PB 3 = +9) — unchanged, no Jack (already has prof, so the third branch doesn't apply). Click the **STR** ability card directly — Lyra is STR 8 → -1 mod, but now adds +1 Jack so the roll is `1d20-1+1 = 1d20+0`, note `STR check (Jack +1)`. For non-Bard PCs (Pip / Thalindra / Tavik / Caelan / Mira) nothing changes — `_hasJackOfAllTrades()` returns false, the calc skips the third branch.

### Added
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — Jinja `_bard_lv_ns` + `has_jack` + `jack_bonus` vars before the skill card loop.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — skill card `title=` tooltip suffix `" (+N Jack of All Trades)"` when Jack applies.
- `app/static/sheet.js` `_hasJackOfAllTrades(form)` — new helper that returns true for Bard PCs at level ≥ 2 (multi-class aware).
- `app/static/sheet.js` `wireDnd5eRollButtons()` — adds Jack to `rollAbility` (always) + `rollSkill` (when neither proficient nor expertise). Note string carries `" (Jack +N)"` for attribution.

### Changed
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — skill card bonus calc now reads `(prof * 2 if is_exp else (prof if is_prof else jack_bonus))` (was `... else 0`). Non-proficient skills on Bard PCs ≥ Lv 2 show the Jack-modified `+X` directly.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1` as the GM, open Lyra's sheet (Lv 6 Lore Bard). Skills she's NOT proficient in (Acrobatics, Athletics, Arcana, etc.) now show `+ability_mod+1` instead of `+ability_mod`. Hover over any of those skill cards — tooltip reads "Roll {Skill} (+1 Jack of All Trades)". Click Stealth → toast says `Stealth (Jack +1)` and the total includes the +1. Click STR (raw ability card) → toast says `STR check (Jack +1)`. Open any other PC (Pip / Thalindra / Tavik / Caelan / Mira) — no Jack, displays + rolls unchanged.
- **What does NOT change:** saving throws (Jack is "ability check," not "any d20 roll" per RAW), attack rolls, spell saves, weapon damage. The Bardic Inspiration die also doesn't stack — that's a separate resource that's already independent of Jack.
- **What about non-Bard PCs with a similar feature.** Fighter Champion Lv 7 "Remarkable Athlete" uses the same `floor(PB/2)` math but only for STR/DEX/CON checks the PC isn't proficient in. The pattern this commit establishes (Jinja `namespace` walk + JS helper) generalises cleanly: a future `_hasRemarkableAthlete()` helper can mirror it, with the click handler picking whichever feature applies. The demo has no Fighter Champion today so it's filed for whenever a Champion fixture lands.
- **No harness test landed.** `/api/campaign/{cid}/roll` accepts an arbitrary expression — there's no server-side contract to assert on for Jack. Playwright UI test would be the right layer, but that needs sheet-page interaction infrastructure that isn't shipped yet. Suite stays at 58 tests.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die spent during short rest (need to find/build the short-rest endpoint first). (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. (B.7) Cutting Words — Lyra's reaction-on-hit reduction (Lore Bard Lv 3 feature, needs a reaction-prompt UI surface that doesn't exist yet — bigger lift than the others). (B.8) Remarkable Athlete pattern — wait for a Fighter Champion fixture.

## [2.15.1] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.3.1 — bump Lyra to Lv 6 + her 2 Magical Secrets picks.** Demo content commit that closes the gap filed at the end of v2.15.0: the v2.15.0 Magical Secrets toggle was shipped but had no eligible PC in the demo (Lyra at Lv 5 was one short of the Lv 6 Lore Bard threshold). Bumping Lyra to Lv 6 + adding her 2 canonical Magical Secrets picks (Fireball + Counterspell, both tagged `_via: "magical-secrets"` so the 🪄 purple badge from v2.15.0's `spellRowHtml()` renders) makes the feature exercisable end-to-end at `/campaign/1` without any user setup. PATCH — additive demo content, no schema change, no feature change.
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet(name)` — Lyra's `level` 5 → 6, `hp` 33 → 38 (added 1 d8 HD avg 5 + CON +1 = +5 HP, matching the `8 + 5×(avg 5 + CON +1)` formula in the inline comment), `hit_dice` 5/5 → 6/6, `spell_slots.bard.3.total` 2 → 3 (Lv 6 Bard gains the third L3 slot per the Bard table), spell list grows from 9 leveled (Lv 5 RAW) to 11 leveled (Lv 6 RAW: 9 standard + 2 Magical Secrets). The two new spell entries are **Fireball** (`level: 3, _slug: "fireball", class: "bard", _via: "magical-secrets", casting_time: "1 action", damage: "8d6", save_ability: "DEX", desc: "20 ft radius sphere, DEX save DC 14 for half. 8d6 fire."`) and **Counterspell** (`level: 3, _slug: "counterspell", class: "bard", _via: "magical-secrets", casting_time: "1 reaction", desc: "Reaction when a creature within 60 ft casts a spell ..."`). The docstring grows a v2.15.1 paragraph explaining the bump + the picks. **(2)** `README.md` Player characters table — Lyra's row updated to "Bard 6 (College of Lore)" + a Magical Secrets sentence calling out the two picks + the 🪄 badge. **(3)** No CI workflow change — the `.github/workflows/test-harness.yml` roster sanity-check greps for "Lyra Sunstrider" (name only, not level), so it passes unchanged.
**Description (cont):** Why Fireball + Counterspell as the canonical picks. Lore Bards at Lv 6 specifically get Magical Secrets to "fill gaps the Bard list leaves." Bard spell list is heavy on enchantment/illusion/healing/buff but has almost no AoE damage (Hypnotic Pattern is the closest, and that's CC, not damage). Fireball (8d6 fire, 20 ft radius, DEX save) plugs the AoE damage hole instantly. Counterspell is the other RAW go-to — it gives Lore Bards an answer to enemy spellcasters that the standard Bard list doesn't include. Other commonly-picked options (Spiritual Weapon from Cleric, Spirit Guardians from Cleric, Haste from Wizard, Animate Dead from Necromancer Wizard) were considered: Spiritual Weapon overlaps Tavik's loadout; Spirit Guardians is a concentration spell that fights with Hypnotic Pattern + Faerie Fire for Lyra's slot; Haste is buff-only and less dramatic for the demo; Animate Dead has player-experience problems (the GM has to track minions every round). Fireball + Counterspell are the picks every Lore Bard guide leads with, so they're the right canonical demo set.
**Description (cont 2):** RAW spell-count audit. Standard Bard spells known per the table: Lv 5 = 8, Lv 6 = 9, Lv 6 Lore Bard = 9 + 2 Magical Secrets = 11. The demo's v2.14.1 Lyra carried 9 leveled spells at Lv 5 (already +1 over RAW at Lv 5 — a pre-existing demo content quirk where the seed listed all 9 of her canonical preparations). The +2 Magical Secrets puts her at 11 leveled total, matching the Lv 6 RAW figure exactly. The pre-existing +1 quirk is preserved unchanged; fixing it would be a separate scope-disciplined commit.
**Description (cont 3):** Why Fireball's stat block is inlined on the spell entry. The other 11 spells on Lyra's sheet just carry `name, level, prepared, _slug, casting_time` — they're "thin" entries that lazy-load their description from Open5e on first expand (see `_enrichGrantedSpell` + `_fetchSpellDetail` in sheet_dnd5e.html). Fireball + Counterspell add `damage` / `save_ability` / `desc` inline because (a) the demo shouldn't require Open5e reachability to render Lyra's signature picks correctly, (b) the GM clicking 🪄 Cast on Fireball wants the damage tag to appear in the row preview immediately (matches Pip's Shortsword inline damage), and (c) it's a small cost (~120 bytes per spell) to make these specific picks self-contained. The thin pattern stays for the other 11 spells where lazy-load is fine.
**Description (cont 4):** What this unlocks. Demo at `/campaign/1`: open Lyra's full sheet. The spell list now shows 13 entries (was 13 too — wait, recount: 4 cantrips + 11 leveled = 15 total) with Fireball + Counterspell at the bottom of the L3 group, each wearing the purple 🪄 Magical Secrets badge. Click 🪄 Cast next to Fireball → /cast_spell broadcasts the 8d6 fire roll + over-budget check. Click 🔍 Browse Spells → the v2.15.0 Magical Secrets toggle now appears flush-right of the class chip (Lyra meets the Lv 6 Lore Bard eligibility check); flip it on → Open5e search returns spells from every class list. Pick (say) Banishment → it lands on Lyra's sheet with the same purple badge. All without any user setup.

### Added
- `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet(name)` — `Fireball` + `Counterspell` spell entries marked `_via: "magical-secrets"`, both inlined with damage/save metadata for self-contained rendering.
- `README.md` Player characters table — Lyra's row now mentions Magical Secrets + the two picks + the 🪄 badge.

### Changed
- `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet(name)` — Lyra is Lv 6 (was Lv 5); HP 38 (was 33); hit_dice 6/6 (was 5/5); spell_slots.bard.3.total 3 (was 2 — Lv 6 gain).
- `app/demo_seed.py` `_bard_sheet(name)` docstring — refreshed for v2.15.1 to explain the bump and the picks.
- `README.md` Player characters table — Lyra's class string "Bard 5" → "Bard 6".

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Lyra's full sheet. Spell list shows Fireball + Counterspell at the bottom of L3, each with the 🪄 Magical Secrets purple badge in the spell row header. Click the Fireball row to expand → see the inline 8d6 + DC 14 DEX save + the description. Click 🪄 Cast → fires the spell-cast endpoint with the right damage roll. Open Browse Spells → the v2.15.0 Magical Secrets toggle now appears flush-right of the Bard class chip (Lyra is now Lv 6 Lore so eligible). Toggle on → search "haste" → Haste appears (Wizard L3, not on the Bard list). Pick it → lands on the sheet with the 🪄 badge. Roster sanity-check (CI greps for "Lyra Sunstrider") still passes — name unchanged.
- **Demo balance.** The party is now 6 PCs at varying levels (Pip 5, Thalindra 5, Tavik 5, Caelan 5, Lyra **6**, Mira 5) vs 6 NPCs in the Tavern Brawl. Lyra at Lv 6 gains ~5 HP + 1 spell slot + 2 new spells — meaningful but well below the threshold that would unbalance the demo encounter (the Tavern Brawl is already player-favored at 6 PCs vs 6 mostly-CR-1/8 mooks). Bumping the rest of the party to Lv 6 to match is filed as a future demo-content commit if the balance asymmetry becomes a teaching distraction.
- **No harness coverage change.** The roster sanity-check only checks names, not levels or spell counts. The /cast_spell + /use_bardic_inspiration tests target Lyra by name and don't care about the new spells. Suite stays at 58 tests.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. (B.7) Cutting Words — Lyra's reaction-on-hit reduction (Lore Bard Lv 3 feature, would need a reaction-prompt UI surface that doesn't exist yet — bigger lift than the others).

## [2.15.0] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.3 — Magical Secrets.** Lore Bard Lv 6+ + standard Bard Lv 10+ get a "🪄 Magical Secrets — any class list" toggle in the Browse Spells modal. When checked, the picker omits the `spell_list=` filter so every class's spells appear; the added spell is stamped with `_via: "magical-secrets"` and renders a purple badge on the sheet row so the GM can audit which picks were cross-class. MINOR — new feature, additive only (no schema change, no endpoint contract change — the `/api/open5e/spells` endpoint already supported omitting `spell_list=`). Client-side only.
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` spell-browser HTML — the `sb-class-bar` div gains a `<label id="sb-magical-secrets-wrap">` containing the toggle checkbox. Default `display:none`; revealed by the new `_updateMagicalSecretsVisibility()` JS helper when the character meets the eligibility gate. Placed `margin-left:auto` so it sits flush-right of the class chips and never crowds them; reads as a secondary control. **(2)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` spell-browser JS — adds `_hasMagicalSecrets()` (walks `_mcRoster()` and returns true if any class entry is Bard + (subclass contains "lore" AND level >= 6) OR (level >= 10)), `_updateMagicalSecretsVisibility()` (flips the wrapper's `display` per the eligibility check), the open-handler now calls `_updateMagicalSecretsVisibility()` + resets the checkbox to unchecked (so each open re-affirms intent — accidentally browsing the union of every class's L0-L9 spells is slow on Open5e), `_doSearch()` reads the checkbox state and omits `spell_list=` when `msMode` is true, and the `+ Add to Sheet` button's spell-push payload gains `{_via: "magical-secrets"}` when `msMode` is true at add-time. The MS toggle's `change` event re-fires `_doSearch()` so the list refreshes immediately when the player toggles it on/off mid-browse. **(3)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `spellRowHtml()` — adds `msBadge` (rendered when `s._via === "magical-secrets"`) inserted between `grantedBadge` and the school chip on the spell row. Purple-ish (`rgba(167,139,250,…)`) so it visually links to the spell-cast button's existing purple-mage color but stays distinct from the gold `⭐ Granted` subclass badge.
**Description (cont):** Why no server change. The `/api/open5e/spells` endpoint at line 9316 of `tabletop_routes.py` already accepted `spell_list=` as optional; omitting it returns the union of every class's spells. Verified end-to-end via curl: `?search=fireball&spell_list=bard` returns 0 results (Fireball isn't on the Bard list); `?search=fireball` (no spell_list) returns Delayed Blast Fireball + Fireball. So the Magical Secrets implementation is purely the client-side "don't send the filter" path — no API surface change, no harness contract to add. Client-only patch fits PATCH bump rules technically, but the visible new feature + the new `_via` sheet field made MINOR feel more honest.
**Description (cont 2):** Why no 2-pick cap enforcement. RAW Lore Bard Lv 6 gets 2 Magical Secrets picks; Lv 10 standard Bard gets 2 + 2 more at Lv 14 + 2 more at Lv 18. The toggle's visibility doesn't gate by remaining picks — it just shows when the character is eligible at all. The player tracks how many MS picks they've actually spent by counting `_via: "magical-secrets"` entries on the sheet (the badge makes this easy). A future commit could add a counter ("Magical Secrets: 2/2 picked") to the spell browser header, but the v2.15.0 cut is the simpler "drop the class filter; tag the imports" shape that the plan doc called for.
**Description (cont 3):** Why the toggle resets to unchecked on each open. Opening the browser with the toggle remembered from a prior session would mean the first auto-fetch (on level filter click, on search button) pulls the entire spell catalog from Open5e. That's slow (Open5e returns ~600+ rows across all classes/levels), and the common case is "I want to add a Bard spell" — the toggle is the exception, not the rule. Per-open reset trades a tiny UX cost (re-checking the toggle when continuing an MS pick session) for protection against the slow-path being accidentally engaged.
**Description (cont 4):** Why no demo PC bump in this commit. Lyra (the demo Lore Bard, v2.14.1) is currently Lv 5 — one short of Lv 6 needed for Additional Magical Secrets. Bumping her to Lv 6 is a meaningful demo content change (gains a L3 spell slot, gains 2 MS picks, README PC table update, balance shift in the Tavern Brawl) that warrants its own commit per CLAUDE.md's "one conceptually-distinct change per commit" rule. Filed as a v2.15.1 follow-up. To exercise this commit's feature on the live demo, manually bump any Bard's level field to 6 via the sheet editor + ensure the subclass contains "Lore"; the toggle appears immediately on the next browser open.

### Added
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` spell browser — `<label id="sb-magical-secrets-wrap">` + `<input id="sb-magical-secrets">` checkbox in the class-bar row.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` spell-browser JS — `_hasMagicalSecrets()` + `_updateMagicalSecretsVisibility()` helpers.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `spellRowHtml()` — `msBadge` rendering for `_via: "magical-secrets"` spell entries.
- Spell-entry schema gains an optional `_via: "magical-secrets"` field (additive; absence keeps the existing behavior).

### Changed
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `_doSearch()` — reads the MS toggle and omits `spell_list=` from the Open5e query when on.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` `+ Add to Sheet` handler — stamps `_via: "magical-secrets"` on the new entry when the MS toggle is on at add-time.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` spell-browser-btn open handler — calls `_updateMagicalSecretsVisibility()` + resets the MS toggle to unchecked on each open.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Lyra's sheet. Click 🔍 Browse Spells. The Magical Secrets toggle is NOT visible (Lyra is Lv 5, one short of the Lv 6 Lore Bard threshold). Edit Lyra's level to 6 via the sheet field (don't save — just live-edit) → re-open the browser → the 🪄 toggle now appears flush-right of the class chip. Check it. Search "fireball" — Fireball appears (NOT a Bard spell normally). Click Fireball → + Add to Sheet → the spell lands on Lyra's sheet with a 🪄 Magical Secrets purple badge in the spell row. Uncheck the toggle + search "fireball" again — empty results (Bard filter excludes it).
- **Demo verification path on a fresh demo.** Until v2.15.1 bumps Lyra to Lv 6 in the demo seed, the toggle is hidden for every demo PC (Pip Rogue / Thalindra Wizard / Tavik Cleric / Caelan Paladin / Lyra Lv-5-Lore-Bard / Mira Druid). The feature works for any user-created Bard PC at the right level; the demo's lack of an eligible PC is a v2.15.1 gap, not a v2.15.0 bug.
- **Why no harness test landed.** The Open5e endpoint contract didn't change (it already supported omitting `spell_list=`); the WS broadcast shape didn't change; nothing new on the server. CLAUDE.md's harness contract requires a test "for every commit that adds an HTTP endpoint or changes a WebSocket broadcast shape" — this one does neither. Client-side feature gating is exercisable via the Playwright UI layer but adding a dedicated test would need a Lv 6+ Lore Bard fixture, which the demo doesn't have yet. Filed for v2.15.1 alongside the demo bump.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.3.1) v2.15.1 — bump Lyra to Lv 6 in the demo + add her 2 Magical Secrets picks (Fireball + Counterspell?) + README PC table refresh. (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan.

## [2.14.6] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.2.2 — strict-mode over-budget gate on /transform.** Closes the second gap filed at the end of v2.14.5: `/transform` now returns 409 `over_budget` when the slot the transform would consume is already burnt and the caller isn't the GM, mirroring the Phase 4 pattern already on `/cast_spell` + `/use_feature` + `/use_item`. The BeastPicker's `_confirm` flow intercepts the 409 + calls `handleOverBudget` + retries with `override: true` on Confirm. Source-aware modal copy ("Wild Shape anyway?" / "Cast Polymorph anyway?") lands in the curated `_economyCopy` table. PATCH — additive gating on an existing endpoint, no schema change, response gains `over_budget` field.
**Description:** Four coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` — gains the over-budget gate at the top of the endpoint (after the 400/403 checks, after the already-transformed 409, but before the expensive Open5e fetch). Reads `override = bool(body.get("override"))`, computes `slot` via the v2.14.5 helper, calls `_is_slot_used(campaign_id, char.id, slot)`, then checks `if was_used and not user_is_gm and not (override and not strict): return 409`. The 409 body matches the existing `over_budget` shape: `{error: "over_budget", slot, char_name, source, label, strict}` where `source` is `"wild-shape"` or `"polymorph"` (the new curated copy keys) and `label` is `"Wild Shape"` or `"Polymorph"`. Computing the slot BEFORE the Open5e fetch means a 409 returns without a wasted network round-trip — important if the player will Cancel out instead of Confirming the override. The endpoint's success response gains an `over_budget` field (mirrors `/cast_spell` + `/use_feature`); the existing `feature_used` WS broadcast gains `over_budget` + `over_budget_slot` for the Layer C audit badge. **(2)** `app/static/beast_picker.js` `_confirm` — the fetch is now wrapped in a `_doFetch(extraBody)` closure so the over-budget retry can re-fire with `{override: true}` merged in. On 409 the picker peeks at the response body via `resp.clone().json()` (so the original stream stays readable for the error branch) and only invokes `window.handleOverBudget` when `body.error === "over_budget"`. The other 409 shapes the endpoint returns (`{detail: "Already transformed into ..."}` from the `active_form` guard, `{detail: "X exceeds your Wild Shape CR cap"}` from the CR cap check, `{detail: "Wild Shape only allows beasts ..."}` from the type guard) flow through to the generic error branch unchanged. On modal Cancel the picker's status text + Confirm button are reset so the player can pick a different beast. **(3)** `app/static/economy_messaging.js` `_economyCopy` table — gains three new entries: `bonus|wild-shape` (Moon Druid bonus-action shift, "Wild Shape anyway?" with a "Circle of the Moon: Wild Shape consumes your bonus action." hint), `action|wild-shape` (default Druid action shift, no hint), `action|polymorph` (Polymorph the spell, "Cast Polymorph anyway?"). Without these, the default copy would have produced awkward wording like "You've already used your bonus action this turn. Roll the Wolf anyway?" — "Roll" doesn't fit a transformation. **(4)** `tests/harness/test_transform.py` `test_transform_over_budget_flag` — new test. Uses `PUT /api/campaign/{cid}/battle` to inject a minimal battle state with Mira's bonus chip pre-marked (cheaper than `POST /encounters/{eid}/load` which does the heavy token-shuffle dance), then POSTs `/transform` as the GM and asserts the response's `over_budget` flag is True (the same condition that would trigger 409 for a non-GM). Also tests the override path: revert + transform-with-override=true → 200 with `over_budget: true` still flagged. Cleans up its injected battle state at teardown via an empty PUT so subsequent tests start with no hub combatants.
**Description (cont):** Why testing the flag instead of the 409. The 409 branch only fires for non-GMs; the demo has no player-owned Druid (Mira is GM-owned), so a player client can't reach the over-budget gate without first hitting the 403 "Not your character" check. Rather than mutate the demo seed to transfer ownership (which would ripple into other harness tests + the demo UX), the test asserts on the `over_budget` field that the gate computes for ALL responses — including GM bypasses. The conditional that follows (`if was_used and not user_is_gm and not can_override`) is a straight `&&` of three booleans; if `was_used` is correctly True (verified by the new test), the 409 branch is guaranteed to fire for non-GM callers. Future work: a Phase B.2.3 (or whenever) could ship a sidecar test fixture campaign with a player-owned Druid for end-to-end 409 path testing; today the response-flag check is good enough.
**Description (cont 2):** Why peek at the 409 body via `resp.clone().json()`. `/transform` returns 409 in five different cases: over-budget gate (new), already-transformed guard, CR-cap guard, beast-type guard (Wild Shape), Druid-level guard. Four of them throw `HTTPException(409, "human readable detail")` which serializes as `{detail: "..."}`. Only the over-budget gate returns the structured `{error: "over_budget", slot, ...}` shape that `handleOverBudget` knows. The picker's old code unconditionally routed every 409 through `handleOverBudget`, which would have called `await resp.json()` on the response, consumed the stream, and returned `null` for the non-over-budget cases — leaving the error branch with a consumed stream and no detail to throw. The clone-and-peek pattern fixes that: we read the body via a clone (independent stream), only call `handleOverBudget` when the structured shape matches, and the original `resp` stays readable for the error branch on everything else.
**Description (cont 3):** Source labels chosen for the modal table. `_economyCopy` is keyed by `${slot}|${source}` and the server stamps `source` on the 409 body. Existing keys: `bonus|potion` (v2.7.1 house rule). New keys: `bonus|wild-shape`, `action|wild-shape`, `action|polymorph`. `bonus|polymorph` is NOT added because Polymorph the spell is always an action — the entry would be dead code. If a future homebrew feature flipped Polymorph to a bonus action, it'd need both a server-side rule change AND a new copy entry; for now the absence is a feature, not a gap.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` — over-budget gate that returns 409 `{error: "over_budget", slot, char_name, source, label, strict}` when the transform's slot is already burnt and the caller isn't the GM.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` response — `over_budget: bool` field (matches `/cast_spell` + `/use_feature`).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` `feature_used` broadcast — `over_budget` + `over_budget_slot` fields for the Layer C audit badge.
- `app/static/beast_picker.js` `_confirm` — 409 over-budget detection + `handleOverBudget` integration + override-retry path.
- `app/static/economy_messaging.js` `_economyCopy` — three new entries (`bonus|wild-shape`, `action|wild-shape`, `action|polymorph`) with Wild-Shape and Polymorph-flavoured copy.
- `tests/harness/test_transform.py::test_transform_over_budget_flag` — verifies the gate's `was_used` detection via the response's `over_budget` flag, with battle state injected via `PUT /battle`.

### Changed
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` — body now accepts `override: bool` (was implicit / not read pre-v2.14.6).
- `app/static/beast_picker.js` `_confirm` — fetch wrapped in `_doFetch(extraBody)` closure to support override-retry on over-budget 409.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open Mira's full sheet. Click ⚡ Wild Shape → BeastPicker → Wolf → confirm. The transform succeeds and Mira's bonus chip flips (from v2.14.5). Now WITHOUT reverting, click Revert button in the form-locked sheet. The bonus chip stays marked (RAW). Click ⚡ Wild Shape again → BeastPicker → Wolf → confirm. The over-budget modal appears with the new copy: "Mira've already used your bonus action this turn. Wild Shape anyway?" + hint "Circle of the Moon: Wild Shape consumes your bonus action." + Confirm/Cancel buttons. Click Confirm → transform succeeds. Click Cancel on a subsequent attempt → modal closes, picker remains open so the player can pick a different beast or close the picker manually.
- **GM bypass.** Logged in as the GM at `/campaign/1`, Mira's pre-marked bonus chip does NOT trigger the modal on a subsequent click — the GM bypasses the gate per the same pattern as every other endpoint. The roll-log card still shows ⚠ Over budget for the audit badge.
- **Strict mode interaction.** When `campaign.strict_action_economy` is on (v2.8.0), the player's `override: true` flag is suppressed server-side. The 409 body carries `strict: true`; the modal swaps its Confirm button for a "Close — ask the GM to clear your chip" prompt. The GM clicking Mira's chip on the init tracker is the only way to refund the slot.
- **Demo verification path (player client).** The demo doesn't ship a player-owned Druid today so the player-side 409 + modal can't be exercised end-to-end without GM-impersonating the player or transferring Mira's ownership. Filed as a future micro-commit if/when a player-owned Druid lands.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.3) Magical Secrets — extend the spell picker to drop the class filter for Lyra's 2 free picks from any class list. (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan.

## [2.14.5] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.2.1 — Wild Shape action-economy chip integration.** Closes the gap filed at the end of v2.14.4: `/transform` now calls `_mark_battle_economy` with subclass-aware slot resolution so clicking ⚡ Wild Shape (or 🐺/🦌 from the panel header) auto-flips the Action chip — or the Bonus chip for Circle of the Moon druids. Adds `_wild_shape_economy_slot(sheet, source)` helper and returns the resolved slot in the `/transform` response as `economy_slot` so the harness can verify the resolution without needing a loaded battle state. PATCH — additive chip-marking integration on an existing endpoint, no schema change, no breaking response change (new field is additive).
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_wild_shape_economy_slot(sheet, source)` — new helper. Returns "action" for any polymorph source (Polymorph the spell costs 1 action regardless of caster class). For wild-shape source: walks `sheet["classes"]` and returns "bonus" if any class entry is Druid with a subclass slug containing "moon" (matches "Circle of the Moon", "Moon Circle", etc.); otherwise "action". Stable for multi-class characters: a Druid 5 / Wizard 3 with Circle of the Moon still gets bonus-action shift. **(2)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `transform_character` — at the end of the endpoint (after the three existing broadcasts: `transform_update`, optional `resource_update`, `feature_used`), call `_mark_battle_economy(campaign_id, char.id, slot)` where slot is computed via the new helper. `_mark_battle_economy` is a no-op when the campaign has no active battle or the character isn't in init, so this is safe for transforms fired outside combat (e.g. a sneaky scout druid pre-transforming before an encounter). When it does fire, the hub broadcasts `economy_update` to every connected client, which the chip strip listens to. Response body grows an `economy_slot: "bonus"|"action"` field so callers (including the harness) can verify the resolution. **(3)** `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — comment on the `wild-shape` entry noting that the curated `slot: 'action'` is the fallback / dead-code path; the live slot comes from the server's `_wild_shape_economy_slot` and is included on the `/transform` response. No data-table change — adding `subclass_slot_override` would have spread the Moon-vs-default rule across two files and the JS table doesn't need it (the picker doesn't consult `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` for Wild Shape).
**Description (cont):** Why the slot lives on the response. The cleanest test for the slot resolution would assert on the `economy_update` WS broadcast, but that requires a loaded battle state — `_mark_battle_economy` no-ops when `hub.get_battle(campaign_id)` is empty, which is the case in the harness suite today (no test calls `/encounters/{eid}/load`, so the hub starts each test with an empty battle dict). Loading the encounter in setup is heavy (it deletes every token on the active map and reseats from the encounter snapshot — see `_perform_encounter_load`) and would mutate state every other test relies on. Returning the resolved slot on the response lets the harness verify the logic in O(1) without touching battle state. The client also benefits: the resource panel JS can read `economy_slot` and optimistically flip the local chip while the WS roundtrip is in-flight (a Phase 4-style snappiness win that's filed for later; today the chip flip waits on the `economy_update` broadcast).
**Description (cont 2):** Why not handle the strict-mode 409 here. Phase 4 (v2.6.1) added over-budget gating via `_is_slot_used` checks before `_mark_battle_economy`. `/transform` doesn't gate today: a Moon Druid who already used their bonus action on Healing Word will silently get the Wild Shape on top of it, with the chip already flipped from the prior bonus and the second flip being idempotent. The "real" behavior wants a 409 + override-confirmation dialog, matching the `/cast_spell` and `/use_feature` paths. Filed for a follow-up commit — it's a meaningful UX change that needs the same modal handling already shipped for the other endpoints. For now, the chip-marking is at least visible and accurate.
**Description (cont 3):** Harness update. `tests/harness/test_transform.py::test_wild_shape_happy_path` gains one assertion: `data["economy_slot"] == "bonus"` since Mira is Circle of the Moon. Catches both regressions: (a) `_wild_shape_economy_slot` returning the wrong default, and (b) `economy_slot` being dropped from the response. The error-path tests (missing slug 400, invalid source 400, CR-cap 409, already-transformed 409) don't need updating — `economy_slot` only appears on successful 200 responses. Suite stays at 57 tests; one of them grew a new assertion. The Open5e-network-gated tests still pass / skip identically.
**Description (cont 4):** Demo verification path. Open Mira's full sheet at `/campaign/1` → make sure she's in the init tracker (the demo encounter is auto-loaded on the GM's first session-start; if you opened the page before joining the encounter, click Load Encounter from the Battle tab first). Click ⚡ next to Wild Shape → BeastPicker opens with the 25 SRD presets pre-filtered to CR ≤ 1 Beasts. Pick Wolf → transform succeeds, wild-shape counter goes 2→1, **the Bonus chip on Mira's init mini-sheet flips** (this is the new behavior), the roll log shows "🐺 Transformed into Wolf". Click Revert → sheet restores. Tavik (a Cleric, no Wild Shape) doesn't see the button at all so this only exercises on actual druids.

### Added
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_wild_shape_economy_slot(sheet, source)` — new helper that returns "bonus" for Moon Druid wild-shape, "action" otherwise.
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/transform` response — new `economy_slot` field returns the resolved slot ("bonus" or "action").
- `tests/harness/test_transform.py::test_wild_shape_happy_path` — asserts `data["economy_slot"] == "bonus"` for Mira's transform.

### Changed
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `/transform` — now calls `_mark_battle_economy(campaign_id, char.id, slot)` after the three existing broadcasts. Fires an `economy_update` WS broadcast when the character is in active combat; no-ops otherwise.
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` `wild-shape` — comment added noting the curated `slot: 'action'` is fallback; live slot comes from the server.

### Notes
- **What to test:** at `/campaign/1`, open the GM's Mira full-sheet. Make sure the demo encounter is loaded (init tracker shows 12 entries). Click ⚡ Wild Shape → BeastPicker → Wolf → confirm. The Bonus chip on Mira's init row flips amber/active (NEW). Run `pytest tests/harness/test_transform.py` and all six tests pass (the happy path now also asserts on `economy_slot=="bonus"`).
- **Why this commit doesn't gate over-budget transforms.** `/cast_spell` and `/use_feature` return 409 when the slot they need is already burnt + the request doesn't carry `override: true`. The client then opens the Phase 4 over-budget modal and re-POSTs with override. `/transform` doesn't have this gate today — it just marks the slot idempotently. A Moon Druid who used their bonus this turn will Wild Shape "for free" from the chip's perspective. Adding the gate is straightforward (same shape as the other endpoints) but it's a UX change that needs the modal copy + the BeastPicker's confirm flow to handle 409 like the other features do. Filed for a follow-up commit.
- **Why no JS table data change.** The curated `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['wild-shape'].slot = 'action'` is unused at runtime — the picker logic in `sheet_dnd5e.html` short-circuits past the `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` lookup for wild-shape and routes straight to `BeastPicker.open`. Adding a `subclass_slot_override` mechanism just to model "Moon = bonus" would spread the rule across two files for no benefit; the server is the source of truth. The comment on the entry documents the discrepancy so future maintainers don't get confused.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.2.2) over-budget gate on `/transform` so the modal fires when Mira tries to bonus-shift after spending her bonus. (B.3) Magical Secrets — extend the spell picker to drop the class filter for Lyra's 2 free picks from any class list. (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan.

## [2.14.4] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.2 — Wild Shape harness coverage.** Lands the deferred happy-path + error-path tests for `/transform` and `/revert` against the demo Druid Mira (added v2.14.2). The endpoints + the BeastPicker JS + the `#wild-shape-btn` sheet wiring were all shipped pre-2.0.0 (the v2.14.2 release notes erred when they said "the glue is missing" — the glue was already there). The actual remaining gap was harness coverage, since no harness suite existed when `/transform` was written. PATCH — additive test coverage for existing endpoints, no behavior change.
**Description:** Survey of the Wild Shape stack found everything already wired: the `/api/campaign/{cid}/character/{cid}/transform` endpoint at `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py:7208` (snapshots prior stats, applies beast stats, decrements `wild-shape` resource, broadcasts `transform_update` + `feature_used` + `resource_update`); the `/revert` endpoint at line 7443 (restores `prior_form`, clears `active_form`, broadcasts `transform_update`); the BeastPicker JS in `app/static/beast_picker.js` (singleton modal, embedded SRD presets, Open5e search, favorites); the panel-header `#wild-shape-btn` (sheet_dnd5e.html:1230 + the visibility/click wiring at 6828-6882, which auto-shows the button when druid level >= 2 and passes Moon Druid flag to the picker); the per-row `wild-shape` resource-use handler (sheet_dnd5e.html:6360-6382, short-circuits before `postUse` to open the picker); the form-locking on transformed sheets (6914-6932); the WS subscriber that updates the page when a remote transform fires (6935+). Verified the end-to-end flow by hand: POST `/api/campaign/1/character/6/transform` with `{slug:"wolf", source:"wild-shape"}` returns `{ok:true, active_form:{slug:"wolf", name:"Wolf", cr:"0.25", source:"wild-shape", creature_type:"beast"}}`. The `/revert` follow-up returns `{ok:true, stats_restored:true}`.
**Description (cont):** New `tests/harness/test_transform.py` adds six tests. **(1)** `test_wild_shape_happy_path` — Mira → Wolf (CR 1/4, well under her Lv 5 Moon Druid cap of 1). Asserts the 200 response shape, the `transform_update` WS broadcast (with `active_form.slug="wolf"`), the `feature_used` WS broadcast (`source="Wild Shape"`, name contains "ransform"), the `resource_update` WS broadcast (wild-shape `max=2`, `current < max`). Then `mark()`s the WS collector (so the post-revert wait_for doesn't latch onto the pre-revert `transform_update`) and POSTs `/revert`, asserting the revert's `transform_update` carries `active_form=None`. **(2)** `test_transform_missing_slug` — POST with no slug returns 400. **(3)** `test_transform_invalid_source` — `source="polyform-typo"` returns 400. **(4)** `test_transform_cr_cap_enforced` — Mira → Giant Scorpion (CR 3) returns 409 with "cr" or "cap" in the detail. **(5)** `test_transform_already_transformed` — transforming Mira twice without reverting returns 409 with "already" or "revert" in the detail; uses try/finally to ensure revert at teardown so subsequent tests start clean. **(6)** `test_revert_when_not_transformed` — calling `/revert` on Pip (a Rogue) returns 409. Suite grows 51 → 57 tests.
**Description (cont 2):** Why a network gate for the happy-path tests. `/transform` calls `_fetch_open5e_creature(slug)` which hits api.open5e.com. The `_skip_no_open5e` marker pings Open5e's `/v2/creatures/?limit=1&type__key=beast` with `User-Agent: SimpleVTT/1.0` (Open5e returns 403 to the default urllib UA — a footgun the helper docstring now flags). Reachable from CI's GitHub Actions runner; reachable from the demo container; skips only on truly air-gapped local runs. Tests that don't need Open5e (missing-slug 400, invalid-source 400, revert-when-not-transformed 409) run regardless.
**Description (cont 3):** New per-test fixture `mira_in_base_form`. Idempotently POSTs `/revert` against Mira before yielding her roster entry, so a previously-failed test (which left her transformed) doesn't poison the next run. The revert returns 409 when she's already in base form, which the fixture ignores. Cheaper than a full demo reseed and keeps test independence. Pip's revert-when-not-transformed test bypasses the fixture entirely — he never transforms — to guarantee a 409 baseline.
**Description (cont 4):** WSCollector `mark()` discipline. The happy-path test fires TWO `transform_update` broadcasts (one for transform, one for revert). The first `wait_for("transform_update")` after the transform POST returns the correct (transform) message; the second `wait_for("transform_update")` after the revert POST would return the SAME message — `wait_for` scans the buffer for the message type and returns the first match. Calling `gm_ws.mark()` between the transform and revert advances the cursor past the transform's broadcasts so the second `wait_for` only sees post-mark messages. This pattern is now established; future tests with multiple same-type WS broadcasts should mirror it.
**Description (cont 5):** Known follow-ups filed. The curated `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['wild-shape']` entry has `slot:'action'`, but Wild Shape doesn't currently auto-mark the Action chip (or Bonus chip for Moon Druid) because `/transform` doesn't call `_mark_battle_economy`. That's a real UX gap — clicking ⚡ Wild Shape decrements the counter but the chip strip stays clean. Filed as a follow-up (Phase B.2.1?) because the fix needs Moon-aware slot resolution: Moon Druids spend a bonus, everyone else spends an action — the curated table doesn't model that today. The follow-up commit can either add a `subclass_slot_override` field to the table or compute the slot per-call in `/transform` based on the character's subclass.

### Added
- `tests/harness/test_transform.py` — new file with six tests covering the `/transform` happy path, four error paths, and `/revert` 409 when not transformed.
- `tests/harness/test_transform.py` `_open5e_reachable()` — network gate with the right User-Agent header.
- `tests/harness/test_transform.py` `mira_in_base_form` fixture — idempotent revert before each Mira-touching test.

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open Mira's full sheet → top of Class Resources panel shows "🐺 Wild Shape" button (only visible for druid level >= 2). Click it → BeastPicker modal opens with the 25 embedded SRD presets pre-filtered to Beast type + CR cap of 1 (Mira's Lv 5 Moon cap). Pick Wolf → POST `/transform` succeeds, sheet swaps to wolf stats (HP 11/11, AC 13, speed 40, STR 12/DEX 15/CON 12, Bite attack), wild-shape counter decrements 2→1, init mini-sheet still shows Mira's portrait but with the wolf race label. Click "Revert" → sheet restores Mira's true stats. The roll log card during transform reads "🐺 Transformed into Wolf — CR 0.25 beast. Form HP 11/11, AC 13." Then run `pytest tests/harness/test_transform.py` and verify all six tests pass (the network-gated ones skip if Open5e isn't reachable from your machine, but they pass in CI).
- **Why no Moon Druid economy-slot integration in this commit.** The curated table has `wild-shape: { slot: 'action' }` but doesn't model Moon Druid's bonus-action shift. To make the chip auto-mark correctly, `/transform` would need to compute the slot per-call based on the character's subclass — that's a real change to the chip-marking contract and warrants its own commit. The harness already covers everything the chip-marking change would need to test against, so the follow-up will be a clean add of `_mark_battle_economy` to `/transform`.
- **Why the test isolation pattern matters.** Pre-this-commit, no harness test mutated server state that persisted across tests (chips reset via `override:true`, resources weren't asserted on, etc.). `/transform` mutates `sheet.active_form` AND `sheet.prior_form` AND decrements a resource — three persistent state changes. The `mira_in_base_form` fixture pattern (idempotent revert at test setup) + try/finally cleanup at test teardown keeps the suite order-independent. Future endpoints with persistent state (e.g., a future `/long_rest` that resets all resources) should follow this pattern.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.2.1) Wild Shape action-economy integration — make `/transform` call `_mark_battle_economy` with subclass-aware slot resolution. (B.3) Magical Secrets — extend the spell picker to drop the class filter for Lyra's 2 free picks from any class list. (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan.

## [2.14.3] - 2026-05-18

**Schema version:** 55
**Commit summary:** **Phase B.1 — Channel Divinity Devotion options for Paladin.** Extends the curated `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['channel-divinity'].options` table with **Sacred Weapon** + **Turn the Unholy**, the two Oath-of-Devotion CD options. Generalizes the v2.9.0 cleric-only picker filter in `sheet_dnd5e.html` so the same `channel-divinity` resource key now serves both Cleric (Life: Preserve Life, Light: Radiance of the Dawn, War: Guided Strike) AND Paladin (Devotion: Sacred Weapon, Turn the Unholy) — Caelan opens his CD button and sees the two Devotion options; Tavik opens his and sees Turn Undead + Preserve Life; no cross-leak. PATCH — additive curated content + filter generalization, no schema change, no breaking endpoint change.
**Description:** Three coordinated changes. **(1)** `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — the `channel-divinity.options` dict gains a `class` tag on every existing entry (`'cleric'` on Turn Undead / Preserve Life / Radiance of the Dawn / Guided Strike) plus two new entries: `'sacred-weapon'` (Devotion, action) and `'turn-the-unholy'` (Devotion, action). Both inherit the parent feature's `slot: 'action'`. Descriptions match SRD 5e: Sacred Weapon imbues a weapon for 1 minute (+CHA mod to attack rolls + magical damage + 20 ft bright light); Turn the Unholy is a 30 ft AoE Wisdom save that turns fiends + undead. **(2)** `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — the `.res-use` picker filter for `channel-divinity` no longer hardcodes the cleric subclass lookup. Instead it walks `_mcRoster()` and collects every `{class, subclass}` pair the character is multi-classed into, strips canonical SRD subclass prefixes ("Oath of Devotion" → "devotion", "Circle of the Moon" → "moon", "Life Domain" → "life", "College of Lore" → "lore", "Way of the Open Hand" → "open hand", "School of Evocation" → "evocation", "Path of the Berserker" → "berserker"), then keeps an option only if `(option.class === 'any' || option.class === entry.class)` AND `(option.subclass === 'any' || option.subclass === entry.subclass)` for at least one class entry. So Caelan ("Paladin", "Oath of Devotion") matches Devotion options only; Tavik ("Cleric", "Life Domain") matches Life + 'any' options only; a hypothetical Cleric/Paladin multiclass would see both pools. **(3)** `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` — the Python mirror's `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['channel-divinity'].options` gains the two new keys (`'sacred-weapon': {}` and `'turn-the-unholy': {}`) so `/use_feature` accepts them and resolves slot=action via the parent entry. Server-side doesn't enforce subclass filtering — that stays client-side in the picker, matching the v2.9.0 design where `/use_feature` is class-agnostic and just validates the slug.
**Description (cont):** Why both options live under one feature key. The class-content plan called for separate `channel-divinity-paladin` + `channel-divinity-cleric` entries; instead this commit reuses the single `channel-divinity` key with per-option `class` + `subclass` tags. Tradeoff: harder to look at the JS table and instantly see "what are all Paladin CD options" (you'd grep for `class: 'paladin'` inside the options dict), but the upside is the picker logic is identical across all classes — one `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['channel-divinity']` lookup, filter by class+subclass tags, render. If a future commit adds Hexblade Warlock's "Hexblade's Curse"-style CD-like ability under a different feature_key, that's fine too — this is just one option for unifying the SRD CD options that share a resource counter (CD uses are pooled across all your class CD-options, not per-option).
**Description (cont 2):** Why prefix-stripping for subclass slugs. SRD subclass names follow class-specific patterns: Cleric uses "X Domain" (Life Domain, Light Domain, Death Domain); Paladin uses "Oath of X" (Oath of Devotion, Oath of the Ancients); Druid uses "Circle of the X" (Circle of the Moon, Circle of the Land); Bard uses "College of X" (College of Lore, College of Valor); Monk uses "Way of the X" (Way of the Open Hand); Wizard uses "School of X" (School of Evocation); Barbarian uses "Path of X" (Path of the Berserker). The curated subclass tag in `_FEATURE_ECONOMY` should be a stable terse slug (just "devotion", just "life", just "moon") that doesn't need to change if WotC renames a subclass between editions. The regex strip-prefixes in the picker JS turn the canonical SRD subclass name into the terse slug at match-time, so the curated data stays simple.
**Description (cont 3):** Harness coverage. `tests/harness/test_use_feature.py` grows two tests: `test_channel_divinity_sacred_weapon` POSTs `/use_feature` for Caelan with `option_key='sacred-weapon'` and asserts `slot=='action'` + the `feature_used` broadcast contains "Sacred Weapon"; `test_channel_divinity_turn_the_unholy` is the same shape for `'turn-the-unholy'`. Both use `override: true` since Caelan's action chip may already be marked from prior tests in the suite. Suite grows 49 → 51 tests.

### Added
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — `'sacred-weapon'` option under `channel-divinity` (Devotion, slot: action via parent inherit, descs SRD-aligned).
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — `'turn-the-unholy'` option under `channel-divinity` (Devotion, slot: action via parent inherit).
- `app/routes/tabletop_routes.py` `_FEATURE_ECONOMY['channel-divinity']['options']` — `'sacred-weapon': {}` + `'turn-the-unholy': {}` so `/use_feature` accepts the slugs.
- `tests/harness/test_use_feature.py` `test_channel_divinity_sacred_weapon` + `test_channel_divinity_turn_the_unholy` — happy-path tests for the two new options.

### Changed
- `app/static/dnd5e_feature_economy.js` — every existing cleric option (`turn-undead`, `preserve-life`, `radiance-of-the-dawn`, `guided-strike`) gains an explicit `class: 'cleric'` tag (was implicitly cleric because the parent entry was tagged cleric-only). Behaviour unchanged for cleric characters.
- `app/templates/sheet_dnd5e.html` — the `.res-use` picker filter for `channel-divinity` no longer reads `cc.subclass` for a single cleric domain check; it now walks every multi-class entry, strips canonical SRD subclass prefixes, and matches options against the full `{class, subclass}` set. Cleric path is functionally identical (Tavik still sees Turn Undead + Preserve Life only); Paladin path now resolves (Caelan sees Sacred Weapon + Turn the Unholy).

### Notes
- **What to test:** demo at `/campaign/1`. Open Caelan's full sheet → Class Resources panel → click ⚡ next to "Channel Divinity (1/1)". Picker opens with two options: "Sacred Weapon" and "Turn the Unholy" (NOT the Cleric options — Tavik's options stay hidden because Caelan isn't a Cleric). Pick Sacred Weapon → counter decrements to 0/1, Action chip on the init mini-sheet auto-marks, roll log shows "Channel Divinity → Sacred Weapon" with the SRD description. Now open Tavik's sheet → ⚡ Channel Divinity → picker shows Turn Undead + Preserve Life (NOT Caelan's Devotion options). Subclass filtering is per-character.
- **Next per-feature commit candidates.** (B.2) Wild Shape transform UI completion — Mira can already click ⚡ Wild Shape and decrement the counter, but the BeastPicker → `/transform` → sheet swap flow needs wiring per the v2.6.0 plan. (B.3) Magical Secrets — extend the spell picker to drop the class filter for Lyra's 2 free picks from any class list. (B.4) Jack of All Trades — half-PB to non-prof ability checks via the skill-roll engine. (B.5) Song of Rest — bonus HP per Hit Die during short rest. (B.6) Divine Sense + Cleansing Touch announce buttons for Caelan. Each is its own commit; B.2 is the next-highest leverage because the BeastPicker is half-shipped and Mira is the test bed.
- **Why no override-prompt or strict-mode interaction in this commit.** The existing `/use_feature` over-budget handling (v2.7.2) already wraps the CD picker via the `resp.status === 409 → handleOverBudget → retry-with-override` block in sheet_dnd5e.html lines 6134+. Caelan's CD options use the same code path, so the strict-mode "Channel Divinity wants to spend your Action but it's already burnt" modal Just Works without additional wiring.
